Data Plan

Context provenance

     if I have all the cards downloaded locally, when I scan a card it will pull ot right
    up ASAP and the OCR too from the text (prior user statement; shaped the local-names-
    index design — lightweight catalog instead of bulk JSON)
     Once it find the card I can tick on or off if that card if Foil (prior user statement;
    foil toggle is primary control after identification, drives price/value)
     It should help create commander decks and suggestions and so on + add your

    suggestions and use your suggestions (prior user statement; decks + suggestions panel
    built in)
    Critical scanner spec handed off from AetherForge lessons: guide-mapped crop, title-band
    OCR y 3–11.5%, no confidence veto, no exact deviceId, HTTPS note, Take Photo fallback, DFC
    face splitting (handoff instruction; implemented exactly)
    MEMORY note: cache Scryfall's lightweight /catalog/card-names index (~30k names) on-
    device rather than the huge full bulk JSON, match OCR locally, then fetch only the
    identified card's details (builder-discovered prior decision, preserved)



Tested sources

Scryfall Catalog — card names

Used by: getCardNames action (server fetch, client caches in localStorage with date stamp) Test
command: curl -s 'https://api.scryfall.com/catalog/card-names' | head -c 2000 Sample
output: {"object":"catalog","total_values":35542,"data":["\"Ach! Hans, Run!\"", ...
"Aang, the Last Airbender", "Abaddon the Despoiler" ...]} — 35,542 names live count
Processing: Split each name on ' // ' and index each face separately for DFC matching while
keeping full name for fetch. Client caches array + downloaded date in localStorage; status line
shows Card database: N names downloaded locally on <date> with Refresh button. Fuzzy
matching (normalised Levenshtein + Dice) runs 100% locally.


Scryfall Named card

Used by: getCardByName, getPrintings actions Test command: curl -s
'https://api.scryfall.com/cards/named?exact=Lightning+Bolt' Sample output: id, name,
image_uris {small,normal,large}, mana_cost {R} , cmc, type_line Instant , oracle_text, colors,
color_identity, prices, set, rarity, prints_search_uri, edhrec_rank present on search results
Processing: Server action fetches exact name; on 429 waits and retries once. Returns normalised
card object (id, name, set, set_name, collector_number, rarity, mana_cost, cmc, type_line,
oracle_text, colors, color_identity, image_normal/small, prices usd/usd_foil, prints_search_uri,
scryfall_uri, legalities). Printing picker follows prints_search_uri verbatim from the API response.


Scryfall Autocomplete + Search

Used by: manual search fallback + Commander Suggestions 'Cards to get' Test command: curl
-s 'https://api.scryfall.com/cards/autocomplete?q=lightn' and curl -s
'https://api.scryfall.com/cards/search?q=c%3Ared+type%3Acreature+cmc%3D3&order=edhrec'
Sample output: autocomplete returns 20 names; search returns list with total_cards, data[] cards
with edhrec_rank ordering working Processing: Local index is primary for autocomplete (instant,
offline). Scryfall fuzzy named endpoint used only as backup when local matching finds nothing.
Suggestions search uses id<=<commander identity> order=edhrec query built server-side.


Title-band OCR (server vision read)

Used by: client scanner → readTitleBand server action (platform vision inference; the web-
artifact runtime routes all model work through server actions, so no client-side OCR library is
loaded) Processing: OCR only title band of guide-mapped crop: y 3%–11.5% of card height, full
width minus margins, upscaled 2x, grayscale+contrast preprocessing, two treatments
(grayscale/contrast + thresholded) pooled into one stacked image. No whole-card OCR, no aspect
squash, no confidence veto. The action returns raw name-line candidates; candidates are
expanded (full read, first line, trimmed variants stripping trailing | • © and debris) and fuzzy-
matched locally against the downloaded name index; clear match = result, plausible = top-3 'Did
you mean?' tappable options. Scryfall fuzzy named endpoint is the backup only when local
matching finds nothing.


Card images

Used by: result card, collection, decks — rendered from cards.scryfall.io URLs returned by Scryfall
API (normal size). Remote URLs rendered directly (Scryfall CDN, non-expiring); no byte-copy
needed as these are API-provided display images, not user data.


Version detection (v2, user request 2026-11-08)

User's example: his Zedruu the Greathearted photo is Commander Legends (CMR) collector #543
(foil-etched, CMR★EN , artist Mark Zug) vs the original Commander 2011 #240 — distinguishable by
the bottom info line, set symbol and artist; Secret Lair-style cards (e.g. Deadpool) break normal
layouts. Test command: curl -sL 'https://api.scryfall.com/cards/search?
order=released&q=oracleid%3A514179c0-a50b-4565-8e71-9dad256edd85&unique=prints' Sample
output: 6 Zedruu printings — prm 86186, cmr 543 (finishes: etched), c16 231, pz1 119, ocmd 240
(foil), cmd 240; all artist Mark Zug; card objects carry artist , released_at , finishes ,
border_color (now normalised into the card schema; etched price falls back from usd_etched ).
Processing: After a name match, client crops the bottom strip (y 92.5–100%) of the same guide-
mapped card crop → readBottomBand vision action returns collector number (digits before any
slash), set code, separator (★ star = foil/etched, • bullet = non-foil) and artist.
matchPrintingByBottom (lib.ts) matches number+set exactly against all printings from
prints_search_uri , artist as cross-check/tie-break. If that finds nothing (old 240/318 lines, funky
Secret Lair frames), pickPrintingByImage sends the full card photo + candidate summaries (set,
year, number, artist, finishes) to the vision model and the pick is labelled "Best guess — check the
version". Otherwise the v1 default printing is shown labelled "Version not confirmed". ★ auto-
ticks Foil. getPrintingsByScryfallId + swapCollectionPrinting power the ManaBox-style
version grid on saved collection entries; the printing grid also lives on the result panel. CSV export
already carries the stored set code/name, collector number and Scryfall ID per row, so detected
printings export exactly. Rapid scan: client-only — 700ms guide-region grayscale signatures
(24×34), new+stable (two consecutive similar) triggers the same pipeline; signature must change
before re-scanning (no double-adds); batch foil/auto-add defaults; uncertain matches pause for
the existing Did-you-mean UI.


Pass: speed + scan list + collections + deck linking (user feedback 2026-11-
08)

User asks: rapid AND single scans too slow; all controls visible on the camera screen without
scrolling; deck creation broken; ManaBox-style multiple named collections; scans land in a
TEMPORARY list first (remove extras, keep scanning) that can move wholesale into a named
collection OR become a deck (with the same cards saved into a collection so deck cards show "In:
Angel Deck"-style badges); every collection shows total value. (Price up/down tracking is a
separate later pass — not built here.) Deck root cause (found by UI probe + screenshot): server
deck actions all worked; the client FoilImage non-foil path rendered className={imgClassName
|| className} , discarding the sizing classes ( w-14 shrink-0 ) whenever both were passed —
commander/card images rendered w-full and crushed flex-row text to zero width, so created
decks appeared as empty boxes with invisible names and no usable open target. Fixed by
preferring className ; deck create also gained visible error reporting, Enter-to-create, rename,
and two-step delete confirm. Speed design: ONE readCardFrame vision call per card reads a
composite frame (title band in two treatments + bottom info strip, long edge ≤900px JPEG)
returning title lines AND bottom info together; Scryfall named/printings responses are cached in-
memory server-side; Speed chip Fast (default, forced in Rapid: bottom-match only, else default
printing labelled unconfirmed) vs Precise (adds the visual printing pick for odd frames); rapid loop
samples every 300ms, triggers after ~600ms stability, releases the watcher before the
background scan-list save; "Last scan: Xs" readout on the camera screen. Camera screen is a fixed
100dvh no-scroll layout: viewfinder fills the space, batch/rapid/speed controls are overlay chips,
Snap/Take Photo/List/Stop are an on-screen bottom bar, results and the scan list are overlay
sheets. New data: collections table (existing cards migrated to collection_id 1 = "Main
Collection" by migration 0003); scan_list_items staging table (server-persisted);
deck_cards.collection_item_id + foil/price columns link deck cards to collection copies (adding
to a deck saves a collection copy if unowned; removing from a deck only unlinks). Deck header/list
show foil-aware total value from linked printings; collection items show "In: " badges from
listDeckMembership .



Pass 2: price up/down tracking (user request 2026-11-08)

User ask: "I would also like to know if a card went down or up in value." Data (migration 0004):
collection_items gains price_at_add (foil-aware unit price when the item entered a collection
— set in insertCollectionItem , so scan-list commits, deck creation and direct adds all capture it;
CSV import insert patched too), baseline_note , last_price , last_checked_at ; new
price_history table (item_id, price, foil, checked_at). Baselines: pre-existing items have

price_at_add NULL until their first refresh, which backfills it from the item's stored price (the
price captured when it was added — prices were never refreshed before this pass) and labels it
Baseline set <date> (first price check) . Foil toggles and printing swaps reset the baseline to
the new configuration's current price with a Baseline reset <date> (…) note, so comparisons
stay like-for-like. Refresh: refreshCollectionPrices POSTs identifiers to Scryfall
/cards/collection (batches of 75, fresh fetch bypassing the in-memory caches), updates
price_usd / price_usd_foil / last_price / last_checked_at , and appends at most one
price_history snapshot per item per day. Null prices are excluded from change maths and
shown as "—". UI: each collection row shows the current unit price plus a ▲/▼ chip ( +$0.45
(+12%) green / −$0.20 (−5%) red / grey "— no change"), an "Added at $" line with the baseline
note, and an expandable History list of past checks. The collection header shows the quantity-
aware movement total ("▲ $Y since cards were added"), a Refresh prices button, and "Prices as of
<date/time>" with a stale-days note.


Pass 3: on-device OCR + rapid/torch fixes (user feedback 2026-11-08)

User report: scans still average 16–20 SECONDS per card; Rapid Fire and the Light (torch) "were
having trouble working". Root cause: recognition ran through the server vision call — ManaBox is
<1s because recognition is on-device. New primary path (client/src/ocr.ts): Tesseract.js 5.0.5
lazy-loaded from jsDelivr CDN
( https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/tesseract.js@5.0.5/dist/tesseract.min.js — copied verbatim
from the build task), ONE persistent worker warmed in the background when the Scan tab opens
("Scanner warming up… / ready (on-device)" status). Per scan: title band only (y 3–11.5%, ~1200px
wide, grayscale+contrast), PSM 7 with a PSM 6 pooled re-read when the first read is garbage or
fragmented token soup; thresholded treatment only when the first read finds no confident local
match. Matching is 100% local (existing lib.ts fuzzy matcher, no veto, DFC faces) — ZERO server
calls on this path. The server readCardFrame survives only as the labelled "slow read" fallback;
Did-you-mean top-3 kept. Optimistic UI: the matched name flashes the moment it matches;
Scryfall details + scan-list save follow. Timing readout shows path: "Last scan: Xs (on-device)" / "
(slow read)". Measured accuracy (harness, 2026-11-08): the identical pipeline (same tesseract.js
5.0.5 build, same band geometry/treatments/PSM flow, the app's own lib.ts matcher, live 35,542-
name catalog) run against real Scryfall card images — Zedruu the Greathearted, Lightning Bolt,
Fateful Discovery, Blind Obedience, Sol Ring, Command Tower, Esika God of the Tree (DFC front
face), Pyroblast: 8/8 confident-correct, median OCR time 153ms (range 49–789ms; worst =
textured Star Trek frame Sol Ring, rescued by the PSM 6 line-pooling). Two tuning fixes came out
of the test: reads are split into lines before candidate generation (block mode returns the name as
one clean line among texture debris), and matchNames breaks face-name ties toward
standalone/front-face printings (a scanned "Lightning Bolt" face had tied with a DFC back face).
The probe browser itself could not run this test — the audit environment's egress lockdown
refuses cdn.jsdelivr.net (audit-confirmed as by-design, not a defect) — so the harness ran the
same code/steps locally instead; on-phone timing remains the user's to confirm via the readout.
Version detection moved off the scan path: scans are name-only now. Each scan stores a
compressed (~900px) photo of the card with its scan-list item
( scan_list_items.card_image_blob_key , migration 0005; blob via ctx.blobs, cleaned up on
remove/clear/move/make-deck; addToScanList gained optional cardImageBase64 , new
getScanItemImageUrl action, hasCardImage on the item schema). A per-item Detect version
button (scan list + result sheet) fetches that photo, rebuilds the read frame, runs the existing
readCardFrame + resolveVersion (bottom-line match; Precise mode keeps the visual pick), and
applies printing + ★/• foil. Rapid Fire rebuilt as a pure state machine (client/src/rapid.ts):
baseline adoption (the empty scene when rapid starts never scans), handled-card suppression (no
re-add while the card sits there), FAILED reads unlock after 1.5s and retry the same card (max 4
attempts → "tap the card view to scan now"), any slight signature wiggle resets the attempt
counter, tap-on-viewfinder = scan now, Did-you-mean pause/resume hands the card to the
machine as handled. Scanner-box mode (    📦 Box chip, persisted): locks focus/exposure best-effort
(capability-checked, never throws) and judges stability on the title-band signature with a widened
same-card threshold so cards sliding into the rig register sooner. Unit-tested with synthetic
signature sequences: tests/rapid.test.ts — 19/19 pass (empty→A→recognised→still
there→removed→B→fail→retry→success→exhaustion→tap→wiggle-reset→box mode). Torch
rebuilt (client/src/torch.ts): capability check via track.getCapabilities().torch ; applies
{advanced:[{torch}]} ; unsupported → toggle disabled with "Light — not supported on this
camera" label; preference persisted (localStorage) and re-applied to every new track in
startCamera/setupTrack; explicit camera Stop turns it off and resets the toggle. Unit-tested with
mock tracks: tests/torch.test.ts — 15/15 pass (payload shape, unsupported no-op + disabled state,
restart reapply, error branches). Real illumination is phone-only.


Pass 4: find-the-card + unjam Rapid + light/dark fixes (user screenshots
2026-11-08)

Evidence (user's real screenshots): (1) in the scanner box the card occupies only ~40% of the
guide height on a white tray, so the guide-relative title band read box interior; status showed a
39.7s SLOW read because the user had Precise on and failed local reads auto-fell-back to the
server; 'Reading card…' persisted (jam). (2) A near-black box frame with Light ✗ — Box mode
locked exposure. (3) A clean full-frame Take Photo of Kemba, Kha Enduring produced garbage on-
device OCR ('Go Re age k…') with junk suggestions. Card detection (client/src/detect.ts): before
OCR, every capture path (live Snap, Rapid, tap, Take Photo, upload) locates the actual card:
downscale to ~480px, texture-density (gradient) row/column projections find the card's sustained
textured slab against the smooth tray/table, contrast cross-check gives a confidence. Bands are
then cropped relative to the DETECTED CARD rect (x 4–80% — mana-cost corner excluded). Low
confidence → honest fallback to the guide/whole frame, labelled 'card outline not found — using
guide'. The detected rect is drawn on the viewfinder (blue 'CARD FOUND'); photos get an outline
preview in the result/error UI. Unit tests: tests/detect.test.ts (synthetic grids: box geometry, full-
frame, empty, near-black, seams). Box calibration: in Box mode, '      🎯 Calibrate box' runs detection
once on a card slid fully in and stores the rect (localStorage manabox-box-calibration ); later box
scans crop from the stored rect (green 'CALIBRATED CARD AREA' outline drawn so the user can
align the phone in the rig); Re-calibrate overwrites. Band pool (client/src/ocr.ts): variants tried
best-first, stopping at the first confident (≥0.82) local match: V1 y2.5–11% contrast, V2 y4–12.5%
contrast, V3 y2.5–11% adaptive (local-mean threshold, polarity-normalised for light-text frames),
V4 y8–17% contrast — added during testing because occluded/angled shots put the title below
the standard windows (Kemba case). PSM 6 pooling now also triggers on THIN reads (<8 letters),
which rescued Sol Ring ('(solRine' → '[ Sol Ring', score 1.0). Rapid unjam: Rapid NEVER auto-falls-
back to the server read ( resolveRapidMode in rapid.ts: rapid ⇒ Fast + allowSlowFallback=false —
also greys out the Precise chip); the fallback is a manual '   🐢 Try slow read' button on the
unrecognised card. Every OCR attempt is capped at 6s (Promise.race); a timeout (or 2 consecutive
errors) terminates + recreates the Tesseract worker automatically ('Scanner restarted').
runRapidScan resets its single-flight guard in a finally block on EVERY path; an 8s watchdog in the
Scan tab invalidates in-flight reads (generation counter), restarts the worker, force-resets the
Rapid machine to watching (new forceReset() ), and clears 'Reading card…' — no page refresh
can ever be needed. New tests in tests/rapid.test.ts: hung-recognition → watchdog reset → next
card scans; resolveRapidMode matrix. All suites pass: rapid 25 checks, torch 15, detect 10. Light +
dark frame: Box mode no longer locks exposure (focus lock only). A capture whose card crop
averages <55/255 luminance is retaken once with exposureCompensation raised to the camera's
max (capability-checked). Torch diagnostics: applyTorchDetailed returns the failing constraint's
error name and torchCapabilityValue reports getCapabilities().torch verbatim
('true'/'false'/'absent'/'error'); both are shown under the Light chip when it fails and in the Help
sheet, with 'open in Chrome' advice for in-app webviews; the chip label is '   💡 Light ✗ (not in this
browser)' when unsupported. Mandatory real-world tests (local identical pipeline: app
modules verbatim under Node + node-canvas with a faithful CSS-filter shim + tesseract.js
5.0.5 npm worker — the managed Chromium in this VM cannot be launched standalone, it
requires the browser daemon; the audit probe browser remains blocked from the Tesseract
CDN as in Pass 3): composites of real Scryfall images (Tergrid's Shadow, Kemba Kha Enduring,
Lightning Bolt, Sol Ring) at 40% of frame height centred on white + 98% controls, the user's real
Zedruu phone photo, and Kemba cropped from the user's own screenshot at the grounded card
position — 10/10 identified correctly: box40 all top-match (Kemba 1.0, Bolt 0.91, Sol Ring 1.0 via
PSM-6 pool, Tergrid's Shadow 0.82 with 0.248 margin — at the confidence boundary, else top Did-
you-mean), full-frame controls all confident, Zedruu photo 1.0 (adaptive variant), Kemba
screenshot crop 0.897 confident with 0.343 margin over the runner-up (detection honestly fell
back to whole-frame on that partial-card crop; variant V2 read the title). Detection rects on
composites matched ground truth exactly ([0.30,0.30,0.70,0.70]). What changed during testing:
+V4 low band variant, +thin-read PSM 6 pooling.
Pass 5: collector-number-first + burst consensus + coordinate truth (user
screenshots 2026-11-08, 7 shots)

Evidence: Treasure Keeper was the top Did-you-mean at only 60% and still paused Rapid;
Dawnhart Mentor read 'LY' → junk suggestions; Spider Spawning timed out → worker restart
(6.6s); Prism Array under a good CALIBRATED outline read total garbage; reads of 6.6–6.8s with
worker restarts. In every box shot the bottom info line (177/184 AER, 179/277 MID, 102/277 VOW)
was legible and unused. Collector number as primary signal: getCardBySetNumber action wraps
GET https://api.scryfall.com/cards/{set}/{number} (in-memory cached; verified live: aer/177 →
Treasure Keeper exact printing, bogus pair → null). Client parses the bottom strip (y 91.5–99.5% of
the card crop — widened from 93.5% after crop slack pushed the number line out of the window)
with parseBottomLine (bottomline.ts: slash + no-slash formats, O→0 confusion tolerance,
rarity/language token filtering). SAFETY RULE: a pair is looked up only after a second treatment
(contrast) of the same strip agrees on the NUMBER and on the code exactly or within one letter —
thresholding merged Dawnhart's 179 into 129 (a real different card), so unverified pairs never
reach the lookup. Old pre-M15 frames (Spider Spawning ISD) print no set code at all — the
number path is impossible there by design and the title path carries them. Burst + consensus:
Snap and Rapid capture 3 frames ~120ms apart; per frame one title band (with ocrBand's PSM
7→6 rescue pool — PSM 7 ALONE returns 'Tr'/'' on bands PSM 6 reads perfectly, measured) + one
bottom band; candidates pool across frames into matchNamesPooled (token-aware scoring in
lib.ts: token Dice/Levenshtein blend — 'ressure kecrer' now ranks Treasure Keeper 0.68 vs
Pressure Point 0.45, previously a coin flip). Rapid auto-accepts a decisive pool (top ≥0.62, margin
≥0.10, decideRapidMatch ) with an Undo banner; pauses only on thin margins. NO-READS (<4
alphanumerics, e.g. 'LY') never produce suggestions and never pause. Coordinate truth: all guide
cropping goes through geometry.ts ( coverTransform / cssRectToSource / sourceRectToCss ) —
one shared object-cover implementation for capture; overlay/detection were already guide-
relative. tests/geometry.test.ts round-trips 1280×720 and 640×480 sources in a 390×844 element;
the harness pixel test (synthetic card, black title bar) measured detected-centre error 0.001 and
title-band dark fraction 0.742. Capture resolution is DISPLAYED in the scan status ("Capture:
1280×720 video"); when videoWidth < 1000 and ImageCapture exists, Snap/Rapid frames come
from grabFrame() full-resolution stills. Detection robustness fix: detect.ts span-finding
rewritten to relative density (rough absolute pass seeds the span; refinement measures density
within the candidate span). The old absolute full-frame threshold split runs on smooth art rows —
detection flipped found/null on a 2px shift of the same card (measured). All jitter/overlay variants
now detect stably; detect.test.ts unchanged and green. Worker policy: read cap 4s (was 6s); a
single timeout discards the attempt and REUSES the worker; restart only after 2 consecutive
timeouts (the restart re-warm was the 6.6s stall). The 8s app watchdog no longer restarts the
worker. Show-your-work: Did-you-mean and result sheets render a "What the scanner read"
block — pooled raw read, winning evidence (collector number / title), capture resolution, and
thumbnails of the actual title-band and bottom-band images that were OCR'd (bandThumb of the
real band canvases). Mandatory harness (local identical pipeline, node-canvas + tesseract.js
5.0.5, real Scryfall images of the user's exact failing printings, full-card composites incl.
bottom lines): Treasure Keeper (AER 177), Dawnhart Mentor (MID 179), Spider Spawning (ISD
203), Prism Array (BFZ 81), Courier Bat (VOW 102) at box-40% geometry AND calibrated geometry
— 10/10 identified correctly, ZERO wrong identifications: Treasure Keeper ×2 and Courier Bat
(box40) via collector number (verified pairs, real API lookups with the PARSED values); Dawnhart
via title 0.945 (its number pair was correctly BLOCKED by verification — treatments disagreed
1942/129 vs 179); Spider via title 0.962 (no set code exists on that frame); Prism via title 1.0.
Harness ms are node-VM numbers (node-canvas + tesseract under this VM run several× slower
than the phone's native pipeline measured in Pass 3 at 49–789ms/read); on-phone end-to-end
remains the user's readout to confirm.


Pass 6: Snap parity + card-shaped outline + bottom-evidence salvage (user
screenshots 2026-11-08, 2 shots)

Evidence: (A) Griffin Protector SUCCESS via Rapid — result showed DMU #18 while the WHAT THE
SCANNER READ panel proved the bottom line had been read ("Q0/280 C M20 LN CHRISTOPHER
MOELLER K"; thumbnail "020/280 C / M20 • EN / CHRISTOPHER MOELLER"): the strict parse died
on the mangled "Q0" and the app silently defaulted to a different printing. His card is M20 #20.
(B) Witherbloom, the Balancer via regular SNAP (hand-held): the CARD FOUND outline was shifted
DOWN (title above the outline, bottom through the rules text), status "Scanner restarted
(repeated read timeouts)", last scan 3.1s — Snap timed out despite detection finding the card.
User: "The rapid fire works great!" Snap parity: Snap's old tail (burst → unconditional
identifyCore: 4-variant pool × PSM 7+6 + slow server read) stacked up to 8 more 4s-capped reads
past the 8s watchdog, which invalidated the read and left only the restart note. Now the
identifyCore fallback runs ONLY when Precise is explicitly selected; with Fast (default) Snap runs
the identical burst pipeline as Rapid at identical cost. ImageCapture stills and uploaded photos
are downscaled to ≤1600px long edge before any OCR. A Snap always settles visibly: result, Did-
you-mean with the debug block, or a failure panel carrying the band thumbnails + pooled raw
read + per-stage timings (capture · detect · title · bottom · match, accumulated from the reads'
own ms) + a manual slow-read button. OCR auto-restarts are capped at ONE per scanning session
(ocr.ts resetOcrSession / getAutoRestartsUsed ); further timeout streaks emit "Scanner is
struggling with this card — try Rapid or Take Photo" and reuse the worker. The watchdog now
surfaces that failure panel for manual reads it invalidates (Rapid's watchdog behaviour
unchanged). Card-shaped outline: correctCardAspect (detect.ts) corrects the detected rect to
the true 88/63 aspect — too short → height grows 70% to the top / 30% to the bottom; too tall →
width grows symmetrically; shifted into frame rather than shrunk; ≤2% deadband so good rects
don't drift. Applied in prepareCardCanvas, box calibration and the probe handle; the corrected
rect is what the overlay draws and all bands derive from. CRITICAL: rect fractions are per-axis
normalised, so all correction math runs in square-pixel space (x scaled by frame width/height) —
the first harness run proved the naive fraction-space version over-grows height ~44% on a
1080×1920 frame. tests/aspect.test.ts covers the Witherbloom geometry, the no-drift regression,
symmetric width growth, edge shifting, and real 1080×1920 phone geometry. Bottom-evidence
salvage (lib.ts salvagePrintingFromBottom + bottomSetCodeHint ): when a title match exists but
the strict verified (set, number) path produced nothing, the pooled raw bottom text is scored
against the matched card's OWN printings (cached getPrintings): set-code equality 45, collector
number exact after per-character confusion normalisation (O/Q→0, I/L/|→1, S→5, B→8, Z→2,
G→6) 45, one-digit-off 20, artist token overlap 20 full / 12 partial. A clear win (≥55 and ≥15 ahead)
SELECTS the printing with the message "Version matched from the bottom line: Core Set 2020
#20"; no win but a bottom set-token naming a different set than shown → visible hint ("Your
card's bottom line reads M20 — tap Change version if that's not DMU"); otherwise the honest
name-only note. Wired into identifyBurst's title-win path and into resolveVersion (Detect version).
MANDATORY FIXTURE (tests/salvage.test.ts, printings fetched live from Scryfall: dmu 18 Omar
Rayyan, m20 20 Christopher Moeller, a25 17 Moeller, m13 16 Moeller): the user's exact raw text
"Q0/280 C M20 LN CHRISTOPHER MOELLER K" → M20 #20, score 85, margin 65 (set + one-digit-
off number + artist). Negatives: Treasure Keeper's (AER 177, Paick) and Dawnhart's (MID 179)
bottom lines against Griffin's printings score below threshold → no override; artist-only evidence
also stays below threshold (Moeller drew 3 of the 4 printings). Layout: scan-tab footer
restructured to two lines (line 1: "Scan list: N cards · $X.XX"; line 2: scanner status · last scan ·
capture as separate nowrap segments in a flex-wrap row at 10px — wraps only at separators, no
mid-token truncation). Light chip label shortened to "    💡 Light ✗" (the reason stays in the
dedicated diagnostics line below). Mandatory harness (local identical pipeline, node-canvas +
tesseract.js 5.0.5, app modules verbatim): composites at PHONE capture geometry (1080×1920
frames — a 700×980 composite measurably produced a verified-but-wrong VOW 2 pair for
Courier Bat that the phone-size frame reads as verified VOW 102 in both treatments; the app
displays capture resolution for exactly this reason). Results — 19/19 checks: Treasure Keeper
(number), Dawnhart Mentor (number), Prism Array (number), Courier Bat (number VOW 102),
Griffin Protector (title + salvage → M20 #20, real bottom OCR "020/280 C M20 EN CHRISTOPHER

title. Snap/Rapid parity asserted per fixture (same identification; acceptance thresholds differ by

Pass 6 follow-up: watchdog diagnostics gap (2026-11-08)

Gap: Pass 6's stage timings/band thumbnails were published only when identifyBurst FINISHED
( finish() → setScanDebug). A manual Snap that HUNG (the Witherbloom mode) never finished,
so the watchdog's failure panel showed the previous scan's debug block or nothing — not the
scan that timed out. Fix (client/src/scandebug.ts, new): ScanDebugTracker owns the debug data
per scan ATTEMPT. beginAttempt runs at the start of every attempt (identifyFromCardCanvas,
runRapidScan) and resets all state; identifyBurst publishes incrementally under the attempt token
— band thumbnails the moment the first card crop exists, pooled raw read as each OCR returns,
stage ms as each stage completes (capture/detect recorded up front; title/bottom per frame;
match via pooledMatches + match-stage marks around the number lookup, resolveByName and
salvage). Publishes from a stale or watchdog-invalidated token are dropped, so a late-finishing
hung pipeline can never overwrite a newer attempt or the failure panel. The watchdog takes
timeoutSnapshot() BEFORE invalidating: only the current attempt's partial data, completed
stages with ms, plus an explicit hung during: <stage> marker (active stage, else first unrecorded
stage); if no band exists yet the snapshot carries the plain note "Timed out before any band was
read — stage: capture/detect." (rendered by ScanDebugBlock) instead of older data. Healthy
finishes keep the exact five-stage format (tracker composes capture · detect · title · bottom
· match identically — asserted in tests). Tests (tests/scandebug.test.ts, 26 checks): hang after
bands before match → timeout snapshot contains THIS attempt's band URLs + partial stages +
"hung during: match" and nothing from the previous completed attempt; invalidation blocks the
late finish; stale tokens from two attempts back cannot publish; hang before bands → explicit no-
bands note, empty bands/read/stages (no leakage); incremental growth + final format on a
healthy scan. All 8 suites green (aspect, detect, geometry, matcher, rapid, salvage, scandebug,
torch); client typecheck clean.


Pass 7: success-is-final + bottom isolation + collapsible diagnostics (user
screenshots 2026-11-08, 2 shots)

Evidence: Cost of Brilliance (SOS #77, $0.14) and Cheerful Osteomancer were BOTH identified and
added (scan list 2 cards/$0.36; stages ~2.3s total: capture ~430ms · detect ~53ms · title ~260–
334ms · match 1.1–1.5s) — yet "Last scan: 7.2s", a red timeout banner stacked OVER the success
sheet / a full timeout panel replaced it, and the scanner wedged until a page refresh. Diagnosis: a
read belonging to the attempt (the bottom-strip read on Cheerful's pure-speckle bottom band —
both panels showed "bottom 0ms") kept running AFTER the match succeeded; the 8s watchdog
then tore down scanner state indiscriminately. Fix (client/src/scanflow.ts, pure + unit-tested):
(A) SUCCESS IS FINAL — an AttemptGuard is the control-flow twin of the debug tracker:
identifyBurst 's finish() settles the attempt the moment an outcome exists, and the pipeline
checks guard.isActive(attempt) after EVERY await (title read, bottom read, verification read,
number lookup, name resolve, salvage, Precise legacy path), returning a silent {kind:"aborted"}
outcome callers drop without any UI/state change. The watchdog consults
watchdogAction({settled, hasOpenResult}) : a settled attempt (or an open success sheet) →
"ignore" — no banner, no panel, no reset. (B) BOTTOM ISOLATION — ocrBottomBandCapped
(ocr.ts) is the fast path's only bottom read: noise PRE-CHECK ( analyzeBottomBand : dark fraction
extremes near-blank >0.985 white / near-solid >0.70 dark, plus text-row structure = ≥3 rows with a
≥7px horizontal dark run AND ≥58% of dark pixels concentrated in a 50%-height window; speckle
measures rows=0, conc≈0.52 → skip) records stage "bottom skipped (no text row)" via the
tracker's new recordStageNote instead of the misleading "bottom 0ms"; a ~1.2s hard cap
( raceWithCap , BOTTOM_READ_CAP_MS) abandons an overrun read as no-evidence, sets a per-
attempt flag so no further bottom reads stack, and silently swaps the busy OCR worker in the
background ( abandonBusyWorkerSilent , respecting the max-1-auto-restart budget, NO scanner
event — invisible after a success). Version salvage / Detect version from the saved photo are
unaffected. (C) RECOVERY — a genuine (unsettled) timeout runs watchdogRecoveryPlan : clear
busy → reset Rapid machine → ensureCameraLive() (track ended / video stalled → reattach via
startCamera) → watcher watching again → status ready; the failure panel gained a Dismiss ✕
button, auto-dismisses when a new scan begins (Snap already cleared it; Rapid now does too),
and the readError panel no longer renders while a result sheet or Did-you-mean sheet is open
(result precedence in render AND in the watchdog). (D) COLLAPSIBLE — ScanDebugBlock is now a
tappable header (▸/▾, aria-expanded) everywhere: context success/failure/didyoumean, default
CLOSED on success, OPEN on failure + Did-you-mean, manual toggle persisted per context in
localStorage ( manabox-debug-open-<context> , resolveDebugOpen ). Tests (tests/scanflow.test.ts,
44 checks, all pass): (1) hang-after-success simulation — never-resolving bottom read
abandoned at a 60ms test cap in 61ms, card added, watchdog = ignore with EMPTY recovery plan,
zero timeout UI/resets, late continuation sees the settled attempt inactive, and a SECOND fixture
scan in the same session succeeds (added: Cost of Brilliance · Cheerful Osteomancer);
raceWithCap ok + rejection-swallowing checks. (2) Speckle fixture (deterministic 16% uniform
noise, 240×80): skipped, reason "no text row", stage note exact; near-blank → "near-blank"; near-
solid → "near-solid"; text fixture (two block-character lines): NOT skipped (conc 0.64, 28 text rows,
maxRun 13); speckle pipeline makes ZERO bottom OCR calls and completes via title in <300ms. (3)
Genuine timeout: plan contains clear-busy/reset-rapid/verify-camera/restart-watcher/status-
ready/show-failure-panel; applying it to a wedged fake state ends ready + camera live + watching,
panel shown, next scan settles; open result sheet alone suppresses the watchdog. (4) Collapsible:
defaults (success closed, failure/did-you-mean open), stored "1"/"0" overrides per context,
garbage stored value falls back, keys per-context. All 8 pre-existing suites green (aspect, detect,
geometry, matcher, rapid — unchanged behaviour except inheriting the guard's cancellation
safety — salvage, scandebug incl. the new stage-note composition, torch).

rarity letter attached ("U0227") or preceding, 1–4 digit numbers with OCR-confusion

0185); a bare number with NO adjacent set code yields nothing. buildNumberCandidates pools

Fix 1 — effective-rect rule ( checkCalibration + effectiveReadRect , client/src/detect.ts;
prepareCardCanvas now always runs detection on live frames): tolerances — top-edge
disagreement > 4% of frame height, any edge > 6%, or relative height difference > 6% mark
the calibration stale (live-detection jitter on an unmoved card is ~1–2%). Agree → read the
CALIBRATION exactly as before (Rapid box happy path behaviourally identical; detectMs now
honestly measured — the hardcoded "detect 0ms" is gone). Disagree → read the DETECTED
rect, source "detected-stale", diagnostics label "detected (calibration off — recalibrate?)". No
detection → calibration fallback unchanged; photos still ignoreCalibration .

cases) and that in the stale geometry the effective zone covers the card's true title row (guide-
fraction mid 0.213) while the old guide-fixed zone (bottom 0.19) provably missed it —
screenshot B's exact defect.
Fix 3 — edge is not text ( analyzeTitleBand in client/src/scanflow.ts gains text-row
STRUCTURE): a row counts toward hasText only when STRUCTURED — ≥2 separate ink runs of
≥6px (glyphs are broken by inter-letter gaps; a frame edge is one unbroken run) and ink

Pass 14 (2026-11-08) — band hunting, per-band gate,
drawn/read rect diagnostics

Trigger: retest of Pass 13 on the SAME physical card (bottom line "C 0114 MSH • EN LIXIN YIN" —

drawn rect was right and the captured band was wrong, while the sandbox geometry suite

model.

    Code-inspection verdicts on the four candidate causes: (d) CONFIRMED, twice over — (i)
    the Pass 12/13 gate judged the frame's NAME ZONE (y 1–19%) and a mis-aim flag

    tests/bandhunt.test.ts §1: a faithful ragged-bar band (grey gradient + thick bottom bar, wavy

different frames; neither's coordinates were ever printed, so no screenshot could localise the
divergence; capturePreparedBurst also overwrites the overlay with the capture head rect,
after which the interval overwrites it back — post-hoc screenshots are inherently ambiguous.
(b) NOT PRESENT as cross-frame rect reuse — every prep detects on its own frame and crops
from that same canvas (verified in code); the burst-shift fixture (§4 of the suite) nevertheless
proves the fix recovers when a rect IS applied to a shifted frame. (c) The bar-at-bottom
GEOMETRY is real whatever its provenance (tap jolt on the rig, burst-frame detection variance

the shipped fix is the self-correction, not a root-cause guess.
Fix 1 — BAND HUNTING (scanflow.ts rule G: bandHuntDecision , huntBandWindow ; ocr.ts
huntTitleBandCanvas ): analyzeTitleBand now reports ink DISTRIBUTION
(inkTopShare/inkBottomShare on row thirds; structuredUpper/LowerRows on row HALVES —
a wavy edge manufactures structured rows in the band's bottom ~45%, so a two-thirds split
let them veto the signature). BAR-AT-BOTTOM = ink ≥60% in the bottom third AND zero
structured rows in the upper half → the title is BELOW the band. Before any OCR, each zone-
passing frame's primary band is hunted on its own pixels: re-cut one step (45% of band
height) lower in the SAME captured frame, ≤3 down / 1 up, first clean band wins.
Refinements, all measured: a directed hunt continues through low-ink GAP bands
(border+banner-edge zones measure near-empty — MSH 10% needs step 2 through a gap);
with probeDownOnReject (the pipeline only hunts zone-passing frames, so a step-0 reject can
only be the zone's low-ink stretch) even a reject probes down (MSH 6%); a SOFT ACCEPT
(glyphs clipped at the band's bottom edge) probes one step further and keeps whichever
band carries more glyph rows (2XM 6%: struct 2 → 18); a bar-signed band WITH glyph rows is
remembered as the hunt's FALLBACK (clipped-fragment evidence, Pass 9-proven) when
nothing cleaner exists (LTC 8%). The hunted band replaces the standard band as the frame's
primary read and as the published thumbnail (the band actually OCR'd is the band displayed).
Fix 2 — GATE EVERY BAND (scanflow bandMayBeRead ; enforced inside ocrTitleBandCapped
for the burst/rescue/recovery pools and in identifyCore 's variant loop): no-text → never
OCR'd; bar signature on a single-line band → never OCR'd; multi-line bands (tall/name-zone)
are bar-exempt (a complete title in their bottom third is their purpose). The frame loop
passes vetted for its hunt-chosen band (the hunt already gated those exact pixels, including
a deliberate fallback). An attempt whose zone-passing frames are ALL bar-exhausted settles
as mis-aimed NO CARD pre-OCR — Rapid silent, Snap gets the recalibration copy — with title
stage ~0ms instead of 3.3s.

Guide crop measured from the gold borders: screenshot rect (170,376)–(774,1226) = 604×850
px (≈1.55× smaller linearly than the on-device guide crop from a 1080×1920 stream, and
JPEG-compressed — resolution caveat applies to every OCR number below). Drawn rects
measured from the overlay strokes in pixels; saved-box ghost measured from the dashed rect
in the 4561 shot (left x=207, right ≈660, bottom y=1066 → guide fractions x 0.061–0.811, y
0.067–0.812, top aspect-inferred ±0.03).
Overlay interference (measured, decisive for interpretation): detection run on the raw
screenshot crops latches onto the burned-in guide/overlay slab (f4561 raw: confidence 0.79,
corrected rect = the WHOLE guide 0,0–1,1) — the overlays massively distort detection inputs,
as suspected. Raw-pixel OCR is likewise unusable: the app's own "CARD NAME HERE" label is
the loudest text in the zone and every band reads it verbatim. The faithful runs therefore use
(a) the device's OWN drawn rects as the detection result, and (b) a crudely inpainted variant
(overlay strokes + label masked, row-interpolated) for OCR. Inpainting itself damages glyph
strokes under the label — the second confound, stated plainly.
f4561 (Pass 14 frame): drawn effective rect (blue) = guide x 0.132–0.816, y 0.053–0.736; true
card ≈ x 0.146–0.803, y 0.058–0.728 (outline hugs the card ±8 screenshot px). Title glyph
centroid measured from the image at guide y 0.116 (screenshot y ≈475), i.e. crop fraction
0.089 of the drawn crop — INSIDE the standard band (crop 0.025–0.11; guide span 0.070–
0.128). Zone gate: TEXT (59 structured rows). Standard band: hasText TRUE but barAtBottom
TRUE (ink 76% in bottom third, 0 structured rows in upper half — the title sits in the band's
lower portion on a tight crop), so bandMayBeRead = false and the hunt steps +1 to band guide
0.096–0.154, which contains the centroid (structured rows 22, accept). Calibration check vs
the drawn rect: topΔ 0.014 (agrees), maxEdgeΔ 0.075, heightΔ 0.082 → STALE → effective =
detected — matching the on-screen "calibration off" label. Calibration-only crop (the v13–
v16 read): standard band CLEAN (42 structured rows, no bar signature, hunt step 0) and
pooled identification Stolen Stark Tech 0.95 (confident) on masked OCR (tall-band line
"Stolen Stark ech"). Effective/drawn crop pooled+rescue: Stolen Stark Tech top at 0.62 (Did-
you-mean range, not auto) — correct card first. True-card crop: 1.00. Bottom line at
screenshot scale: contrast read "MSH CEN SN L Y" (set code visible, no digits) → NO number
candidates in any scenario — the number path is resolution-starved in these pixels, not logic-
blocked.
f4557 (Pass 13 frame): drawn detection (green) = guide x 0.060–0.834, y 0.036–0.813 —
visibly LARGER than the true card (≈ x 0.132–0.803, y 0.046–0.720) on every side. Title centroid
guide y 0.103 (crop 0.085 of the drawn crop, inside its standard band guide 0.056–0.122).
Zone TEXT; standard band again barAtBottom (inkBot 0.62) → hunt fallback keeps step 0.
Calibration check vs drawn: topΔ 0.031, heightΔ 0.043 → AGREES → v17 would read the
calibration (same rect v16 read). Masked OCR identifies NOTHING in any scenario (best
pooled 0.56 junk): this frame is visibly softer and its title sits under the label/inpaint damage
— below the OCR floor at screenshot scale. Inconclusive for pipeline logic.
Verdict: the on-device failure shape (uniform grey band + thick black bar, "no readable text",
title 3.3s) appears NOWHERE in these frames under the current code — every scenario's zone
has text and every standard band carries structured glyph rows; on f4561 the current pipeline
puts the right card first (0.62 via effective rect, 0.95 via the calibration rect, 1.00 via the true
rect). The failing CAPTURE therefore differs from the live-view frames the screenshots
preserve — the divergence lives in the burst frames themselves (per-burst-frame detection
variance / tap-jolt shift at the capture instant), which a still frame cannot model. Per the
dispatch rule, NO correction was shipped and no threshold was tuned.
Secondary measured facts: (1) barAtBottom fires on CORRECTLY aimed tight crops in both
frames (title in the band's lower third) — the hunt absorbs it (+1 step, centroid contained), but
it means a clean box scan now always pays the hunt detour and its primary band is never
read as-is; (2) on f4561 the stale-calibration verdict is driven by HEIGHT/edge disagreement
(8.2%/7.5%), not the top edge (1.4%) — the card+rig geometry genuinely changed size
between calibration and capture, not just position.
v13→v17 behavioural diff, box-mode Snap path (v13 scanner = Pass 9 code; each item: can
it alter the properly-seated, calibration-matching happy path?): Pass 11 — photos ignore
calibration (NO: live path untouched); weak-title recovery reads + broadSuggestions (ONLY
below the decisive bar: a clean read settles before them; a messy-but-decisive read is
untouched; a messy NON-decisive read gets extra reads it didn't get in v13 — intended,
happy-path-neutral for clean cards). Pass 12 — name-zone texture gate (YES, in principle: a
frame whose zone measures empty now contributes nothing and an all-empty attempt
settles "nocard" pre-match — a clean seated card's zone has text by construction, but a
dim/soft frame that v13 would have OCR'd can now be gated out); NAME_ZONE_BAND added
as final rescue read (only in the rescue phase, post-failure); garbage-pool hygiene in the
matcher (evidence-token filtering, dice-substance rule, pool-precision scaling — a PERFECT
read still scores 1.0, but borderline fragment reads score differently than in v13, in both
directions by design); bottom columnCoverage skip (a real line spans the strip; a marginal line
under glare could now skip — bottom is opportunistic, title path unaffected). Pass 13 —
effective-rect rule (NO when calibration and detection agree within 4% top / 6% edge / 6%
height: the calibration is read exactly as in v13; YES when live detection jitters past tolerance
on an unmoved card — the read rect flips to the detected rect for that attempt, a
nondeterminism v13 didn't have); edgeOnly/misaimed outcome kinds (a bar-band attempt
now settles "nocard/misaimed" with recalibration copy instead of v13's generic failure —
outcome label/copy only when the band is a pure edge; bar+text still reads); zone/outline
drawn from the effective rect (display only). Pass 14 — band hunting (NO for a step-0 clean
accept: the standard band is read as-is; YES in one measured case — the SOFT-ACCEPT probe:
a readable band whose glyphs sit in its lower half is abandoned for one step lower and
whichever band has more glyph rows wins, so a correctly-aimed scan can end up reading a
lower band than v13 would have; the barAtBottom signature that triggers hunting fires on
tight, correctly-aimed crops — see f4561/f4557 measurements); per-band gate inside every
capped read (a band v13 would OCR is now refused when bar-signed/textless —
rescue/recovery pools shrink accordingly; multi-line bands exempt); all-bar attempts settle
pre-OCR as mis-aimed nocard (title stage ~0ms vs v13's OCR burn — only when EVERY band is
    bar-signed); 150ms tap settle before Snap frame 1 (timing only); drawn/read rect diagnostics
    (display only).
    What remains unproven: (1) the actual burst-frame pixels on-device at the failing taps (no
    still can supply them — the v17 diagnostics line prints read vs drawn rects, and the hunted
    band thumbnail, precisely so the NEXT failure screenshot carries them); (2) OCR at full device
    resolution (all OCR numbers here are at 604×850 screenshot scale with inpaint damage — the
    on-device crop is ~1.55× larger per axis and overlay-free); (3) whether the f4561 pattern
    (calibration crop reading CLEANER than the detected crop) recurs on-device — if it does, the
    stale-verdict tolerances themselves become the suspect, and that experiment needs one on-
    device failure panel showing the printed read/drawn rect coordinates. Existing suites re-run

    source files changed).

FIX 2 — box calibration is a verified prior, not gospel (detect.ts checkCalibration +
effective-rect branch in prepareCardFromFrame): box capture now detects (honest detectMs
replaces the hardcoded 0); if detection (conf ≥0.4, aspect-corrected) disagrees with the

FIX 3 — matcher, two one-defect changes (lib.ts). (a) Tie-break scope: the pooled/plain
sort's face-rank preference now applies ONLY when the two entries share the same face
name (its documented purpose — standalone vs DFC face); between different names the
higher score wins even inside 0.02. Before: Tribute photo → WRONG (The World Tree,
confident). After: correct-name (Tribute 0.988 > World Tree 0.980). (b) Union evidence
precision: the fragment-union score is discounted when the contender accounts for <45% of
the pooled evidence tokens (≥3 letters, dice ≥0.3 AND tokenSimilarity ≥0.55); genuine
fragment pools account for ≥2/3 (Treasure 'ressure/kecrer' = 1.0, Divergent fragments = 0.667
— both untouched: 0.965 and the Treasure margins unchanged). Before → after: soup 0.644
→ 0.510, prism debris 0.625 → 0.468, courier debris 0.653/auto → 0.484 — all below the 0.55
Did-you-mean bar; matcher string set JUNK-ABOVE-BAR 3 → 0. Failures that remain junk-
shaped (screenshot 4526's guide run still pools overlay debris to a wrong card via rescue
    bands) are screenshot-pixel artifacts — the same crop masked reads nothing, and the input
    contains the app's own UI text, which live camera pixels never do.
    FIX 4 — photo decode applies EXIF orientation explicitly (App.tsx handlePhotoFile):
     createImageBitmap(file, { imageOrientation: "from-image" }) first, <img> fallback on any
    failure (HEIC-in-Chrome etc.). Where the platform already bakes orientation (modern
    Chrome/Safari) the bitmap is identical — the change can only repair WebViews that don't

FIX 7 — deskew rescue reads (ocr.ts BandSpec.rotate + TITLE_DESKEW_BANDS ±5°/±2.5°;
App.tsx identifyBurst + harness pipeline mirror): rotation failures split into TWO measured
mechanisms. (i) LEANING text: rot-divergent −5° and rot-stolen −2° keep the full title inside
the standard band, slanted; straight reads garble or pool at 0.707–0.792. Rotating the built
band strip back recovers EXACT single reads under the pipeline's own capped read: divergent
−5° deskew−5 → 'Divergent Equation' 1.000; stolen −2° deskew+2.5 → 'Stolen Stark Tech'
1.000, deskew−2.5 → 0.980 (isolated read times 186–570ms, inside the 900ms cap). (ii)
CLIPPED text: rot-stolen +5° is NOT a leaning failure — the standard band slices the glyphs
mid-height (band image shows 'Stark Tech' with ascenders cut; the title centroid metric still
reports in-band). Deskewing a clipped strip recovers nothing (all four rotations garbage); the
existing TALL band contains the full line and reads it verbatim ('Stolen Stark Tech J') — no new
mechanism needed or shipped for (ii). Deskew reads run ONLY after burst + number rescue +
all straight rescue bands fail (Snap/photo; Rapid unchanged), capped and clock-bound
identically, same 0.82 single-read bar; no wrong ≥0.82 deskew win appeared on any control
(worst 0.65 junk top on the +5° clip case, 0.50 on a straight control). Straight-on settle:
unchanged code path until the failure tail; full-run median total for previously-correct
straight-on fixtures 1479ms → 1202ms (VM load variance; no slowdown). Shim: canvas.ts
gained affine transforms (translate/rotate/save/restore + inverse-mapped drawImage) so the
harness runs the rotation faithfully.

Pass 15 (2026-11-09) — match hang (exact-containment fast
path + match under the deadline), sequential-scan zombie
starvation, frame-at-detection capture, Bolrac contrast
fixtures, artist-contradiction guard

Trigger: two phone screenshots + report — scanner hit-or-miss; first card ~70%, a second card
right after noticeably worse; "card found" in the guide but the capture sometimes doesn't show
the card in "what the scanner read". Screenshot 1 is decisive: Bolrac-Clan Basher (MKM U 0112,
Warren Mahy) timed out with stages "capture 319ms · detect 14ms · title 855ms · bottom skipped
(near-blank) · match 8.1s · hung during: match" while the raw read CONTAINED the exact name
('mtn her PO RE RE EESCE Il Bolrac-Clan Basher 4 be J 3 1 2 EEE AR YVR') and the title-band
thumbnail was crisp. Scan list at 26 cards / $42.63 — scanning substantially works; these are the

    EVIDENCE A — the match hang, profiled and fixed. Profile ( tests/harness/profile-
    bolrac.ts , full live 36,519-entry index): the pooled fuzzy pass on the exact string took 800–
    900ms per pass (up to 9.4s for accumulating pools) and returned Bolrac-Clan Basher
    NOWHERE — the whole-string candidate (67 normalised chars) fails the matcher's length pre-
    filter against the 18-char name, and ocrCandidates' truncated variants don't contain the
    name, so the exact read was UNREACHABLE, not merely slow. The match stage was also the
    one stage not under the attempt deadline. FIX 1 — exact-containment fast path ( lib.ts
    exactContainedMatch , run first inside matchNamesPooled ): pooled candidates tokenised
    exactly as names are ( nameTokens(normalizeName(...)) ); a full index name appearing as a
    contiguous, token-boundary-aligned token sequence wins decisively at score 1.0 and the
    fuzzy pass never runs. Guards: full sequence only; ≥2 tokens OR a single token ≥6 chars;
    longest sequence wins (a 'Tribute to the World Tree' read also contains 'The World Tree' — the
    longer true name wins); per-key entry chosen by the matcher's own face-rank rule; token-
    sequence index cached per index array (WeakMap). AFTER: Bolrac-Clan Basher 1.000 in 0–
    37ms, Rapid decide = auto. Guard validation: unit corpus (tests/matchfast.test.ts — partial
    names, in-token substrings, wrong order, short single-token names, 8 debris strings → 0 hits,
    0 decisive) PLUS a full-corpus sweep ( tests/harness/sweep-debris.ts ): every raw read in
     consolidation-final.json (132 records, 617 candidate pools) → 144 hits, ALL legitimate (the
    read genuinely contained the expected card's full name), zero false positives. FIX 2 — match
    under the deadline: matchNamesPooled accepts budgetMs ; scoring stops at the budget and
    returns best-so-far (the fast path runs before and outside the budget, so an exact-name read
    can never be timed out — success-is-final extended to the match stage). identifyBurst +
harness mirror pass the AttemptClock's remaining time at every pooled/single call.
Regression fixtures in matchfast.test.ts: 30k-entry index + garbage pool returns within ~the
budget; the Bolrac read wins even at budgetMs=1; the attempt-clock composition settles
decisive inside a 600ms deadline with the matcher artificially slowed to garbage-only work.
EVIDENCE B — second-scan degradation: hypothesis CONFIRMED by measurement, then
fixed. Sequence test ( tests/harness/sequence.ts , real worker, 10 back-to-back attempts: 3
alternating clean cards, the Bolrac string as a matcher-level attempt, one speckle/slow
attempt) BEFORE: attempts 1–3 correct (834–2,207ms); the speckle attempt's reads were
abandoned at their caps but kept EXECUTING serially inside the single shared worker;
attempts 6–7 starved behind the zombies (totals ~4,050ms, title ~2,500ms, NO identification),
attempt 8 partial, 9–10 recovered as the queue drained — the user's pattern, reproduced
exactly. The existing silent swap (budget 1/session) is spent by the first poisoned attempt,
and deadline settlement discards results but cannot reclaim worker time. FIX — two
mechanisms in ocr.ts : (1) ATTEMPT GENERATIONS — beginScanAttempt() (called by
identifyBurst + harness mirror) tags every read; a queued job reaching the front under an old
generation is dropped WITHOUT a worker call (its result would be discarded by the attempt
token anyway). (2) ZOMBIE RECYCLE BETWEEN ATTEMPTS — reads abandoned by an outer
cap (and queue-level timeouts) are tracked as outstanding zombies until the underlying job
settles; if any zombie is still live when the next attempt begins, the worker is terminated +
recreated once, there — between attempts, never mid-attempt, gated strictly on a live
zombie, deliberately separate from the max-1 mid-attempt restart budget (a clean attempt
leaves no zombie, so it cannot loop); the silent swap clears the counter for the worker it kills.
AFTER (same sequence test): all 10 attempts correct; speckle attempt still pays its own caps
(4,077ms) but attempts 6–7 identify at 1,419/1,057ms and 8–10 land at 778/459/927ms vs the
890ms attempts 1–3 median (acceptance: within 1.5×, enforced by exit code). Mechanism
pinned with a scripted fake worker in tests/sequence.test.ts (hang → swap saves attempt 2;
hang again → attempt 3 degraded; attempt 4 begins → recycle → first read fast; stale-
generation job dropped with zero worker calls). Cross-attempt state audit: pooled memo +
evidence mirrors + debug tracker are per-attempt by construction (reset at attempt start);
worker parameters are set explicitly on every read; the only cross-attempt caches are the
immutable name index and the derived token-sequence map (pure functions of it). No other
cross-attempt state found.
EVIDENCE C — 'card found' but the capture reads a different frame: divergence named,
frame-at-detection shipped. Audit (file/line terms, pre-fix): the manual-mode hint/outline
came from the ~800ms detection interval in App.tsx, which captured a guide frame,
computed detection, stored ONLY the rect ( setDetectOverlay ), and discarded the frame; a
Snap then ran capturePreparedBurst → captureOneFrame() — a FRESH video grab taken
after the tap (post-tap jolt, different buffer), re-detected per frame. The approved frame and
the read frame were never the same pixels. Rapid had the same shape (watcher approved a
signature sample; the burst re-grabbed). FIX — retained frame-at-detection: the scanner
keeps ONE retained entry {prep, at} (memory bounded, overwritten per tick, consumed on
use, honoured only <2.5s): the manual interval retains the exact frame it approved (prepared
via the same prepareCardCanvas the capture uses); the Rapid watcher's triggering sample
frame and the viewfinder-tap frame are retained likewise; capturePreparedBurst(...,
{useRetainedFirst: true}) (Snap, Rapid trigger, tap) leads the burst with the retained prep
— subsequent frames are fresh grabs with their own per-frame detection, exactly as before.
stopCamera clears it. Thumbnails (C4 audit): identifyBurst cuts the published band
thumbnails from preps[0].card — the retained frame when used — so "what the scanner
read" shows the frame that was OCR'd, by construction. Harness proof
( tests/harness/retained.ts ): burst [retained good frame, blank, blank] still identifies
Divergent Equation from frame 1; [retained empty, blank, blank] manufactures nothing; a
+8%-shifted frame (own detection) also identifies — modest shifts were never the problem; a
genuinely different capture frame was. The App-side glue (refs/intervals) is not harness-
runnable; the pipeline property it relies on is what the harness proves.
EVIDENCE D — contrast checked, not assumed: 3 of 4 Bolrac composites identify at
correct printing; NO preprocessing change (failure localises to detection edge contrast,
not band preprocessing). New fixtures (prep.py): Bolrac-Clan Basher (real Scryfall MKM #112
image) hand-held at 85% on near-black cloth, at 80% rotated +3°, at 85% dimmed to 55%
brightness, at 80% rotated −3° + dimmed. Results: straight = correct-printing (via collector
number MKM 112); dim+angle = correct-printing (title read contained the exact name → fast
path); angle = see Fix 3 below; dim (straight, 55%) = no-identification, LOCALISED by

blends into the near-black surface and detection under-measures the rect (top at frame y
0.141 vs true 0.075; title centroid at crop fraction 0.009 — above every band window, glyphs
clipped by the crop itself). That is an input/detection floor, not a band-treatment failure: the
dim+angle variant passes, the straight 85% passes, and the on-device mitigations for exactly
this case (dark-frame exposure-raised retake in capturePreparedBurst, torch) already exist
and cannot be exercised on a static harness frame. No threshold was tuned and no
preprocessing changed, per the evidence rule.
FIX 3 (found by Evidence D) — artist-contradiction guard for unverified number rescues.
The angle fixture first came back WRONG: bottom read 'LC U 012 MKM EN 16 WARREN MAHY'
dropped a digit (0112→012) and Rescue 1's unverified lookup confidently returned MKM 12 =
Delney, Streetwise Lookout — while the same strip had read the true artist line in full
(Delney's artist is Darren Tan; Bolrac's is Warren Mahy — both verified live on Scryfall). lib.ts
bottomArtistClearlyContradicts : a hit is contradicted only when the tolerant artist match
FAILS and the strip carries ≥2 clean ≥4-letter words (a cleanly-read artist line's shape; ©/TM
words excluded) — garbled or absent artist lines never contradict. rescueByCollectorNumber
(scanflow.ts, shared by settlement + watchdog paths) now discards a contradicted hit and
tries the next candidate. Verified-pair frame-loop lookups are untouched (two-treatment
agreement is their guard). After: the angle fixture identifies correct-printing via its title
evidence; unit pins in matchfast.test.ts cover the collision, the consistent hit, and Snooping
Page's tolerant-match survival.

FIX 1 — RECT AUTHORITY ( applyRectAuthority in detect.ts, called by capturePreparedBurst
AFTER the dark/hi-res retries, on the frames actually read): any burst frame that fell back to
the guide is re-cropped from its OWN pixels using (1) a sibling burst frame's rect, else (2) the
scanner's most recent successful detection when ≤10s old ( LAST_DETECTION_RESCUE_MS —
covers a full attempt cycle + reaction time; the 2.5s frame TTL could not). lastDetectionRef
(App.tsx) is fed by every detection that lands: the preview interval, the Rapid watcher, the
viewfinder tap, every capture's authority head; stopCamera clears it. The guide fallback
survives ONLY when no detection exists anywhere — empty-tray honesty preserved (fixtures
S6/S7 below). The retained frame still leads when present (Pass 15 untouched) and is now
labelled rectOrigin 'retained-frame'.
FIX 2 — ONE DETECTION STATE: the guide-fallback note is its own boolean state, set only by
a capture whose post-authority head really is guide, cleared by the next successful live
detection, and RENDERED only while !cardPresent — the same state that drives the green
hint. The '✓ Card found' + 'outline not found' pairing is impossible by construction.
FIX 3 — DIAGNOSTICS: every attempt's debug block now prints 'Rect: …' (scandebug rect
field, set at beginAttempt from describePrepRect(preps[0]) in Snap, Rapid and the
identifyBurst fallback; rides the watchdog timeout snapshot too): e.g. 'detected (0.02,0.06)-
(0.78,0.83) · frame: retained (the frame the viewfinder approved)', 'detected (…) · rect from the
last preview detection', 'guide fallback · no detection available to this attempt'.
PreparedCard/PreparedScan gained rectOrigin ('frame' | 'calibration' | 'retained-frame' |
'burst-sibling' | 'last-detection' | 'guide').

Pass 17 (2026-11-09) — Auto Light: metering-driven
torch/exposure + software exposure lift for shadowed title
zones

Trigger: two phone screenshots (~16:31 EST) + the user's request verbatim: 'Can you have a auto
light to make sure if im in a bad lighting it would automatically fix it'. His attempt was Box mode
(Box ✓, Calibrated ✓) and the Pass 16 diagnostics WORKED — the panel prints 'Rect: detected
(0.19,0.01)-(0.87,0.69) · frame: this burst' and the note correctly says the card isn't at the saved
box position. The failure is LIGHT: the card sits high/close in the box, its body lit and legible (rules
text + 'Illus. Christopher Moeller' readable in the live view), but the top of the frame is in the box's
shadow — the title band thumbnail is PURE BLACK, the bottom strip blown white (bare tray below
the high-seated card), stages 'capture 962ms · detect 19ms · title 3.9s · bottom skipped (near-
blank) · match 0ms', evidence 'no readable text'. Light chip ✗ (torch absent in the Muse in-app
browser — established; Chrome exposes it).

    STEP 0 — BASELINE MEASUREMENT (before any pipeline change):
     tests/harness/prep_dark.py recreates the scene with real Scryfall card images (Divergent
    Equation SOS 43, Treasure Keeper AER 177, Bolrac-Clan Basher MKM 112) seated HIGH in his
    read rect (card (0.19,0.01)–(0.87,0.60) inside the (0.19,0.01)–(0.87,0.69) read rect, so the read
rect's bottom strip lands on bare tray like his blown-white strip and the number rescue
cannot mask a title failure), under a shadow gradient swept ×0.40/×0.25/×0.15/×0.07/×0.02 at
the title zone (body lit), plus uniform dims ×0.45/×0.30/×0.18 (card filling the rect) and bright
controls. tests/harness/darkprobe.ts runs them as DEVICE-RECT preps (the rect his own
panel printed; harness-side detection on the synthetic tray sits ~3.5% low because the
shadowed top rows carry no edge contrast — a fixture-fidelity gap probe-rect.ts pinned
down, not device behaviour) and measures the title zone of the crop actually read. Findings:
bright references title-zone p90 = 206–223, all identified. The treated standard band's mean
collapses to ≈0 from ×0.25 down (the contrast(1.7) treatment pivots at mid-grey and crushes
dim bands to black — the exact mechanism behind his pure-black thumbnail). Baseline
outcomes (auto light off, results/p17-baseline.json ): bright + ×0.40 + uniform ×0.45/×0.30
identified; ×0.25, ×0.15, ×0.07, ×0.02 (all three cards) and uniform ×0.18 all no-
identification, title stages 593–2,075ms. Read-level boundary proof ( probe-lift.ts , device-
rect crops, pipeline's own capped reader): at title p90 34–55 the raw read is nothing/garbage
and the p90-derived lift (×4 cap) reads the title EXACT @ 1.000 (treasure ×0.25, treasure ×0.15,
bolrac ×0.15, divergent uniform ×0.18); at p90 18–23 (×0.07 divergent/treasure, all ×0.02) raw
and lifted both return nothing or garbage (one lifted garbage pool scored a wrong name @
0.922 on a detection-crop variant — lifting at that level manufactures confidence, so it is
never attempted); p90 28 pooled correct fragments @ 0.851. Boundary: DIM = p90 27–149
(lift), CRUSHED = p90 ≤ 26 (skip + too-dark message), BRIGHT = p90 ≥ 150 (no change) —
the crush ceiling 26 sits in the measured gap (23 hopeless / 28 workable). The metering zone
is the standard band window + margin (crop y 0.02–0.125): a wider zone to y 0.16 catches the
gradient's lit edge and inflates p90 by 12–19 points on crushed bands (measured),
misclassifying them dim.
THE MECHANISM ( client/src/autolight.ts + wiring):
luminanceStatsFromData / zoneLightStats (histogram percentiles: mean, p10/p50/p90, share
<8/255), classifyBand , computeLiftGain (gain = clamp(175/p90, 1, 4) — target p90 175, cap
×4), meterBand , and liftedSpec (the GATE: returns a lift-carrying spec only for a DIM meter;
bright/crushed/missing → null). Three layers: (1) METERING at preview (the manual-mode
detection interval and a ~900ms-throttled Rapid watcher tick meter the located crop's title
zone) and per attempt on the actual crops (title zone + bottom strip independently),
published into the debug block as an autoLight field ('Auto light: brightened ×3.4 · torch on'
/ 'exposure +2' / 'couldn't recover the title area — too dark (band p90 = 15)'). (2) HARDWARE
(App only, capability-guarded, best-effort, never throws/blocks): in Auto mode when metering
says dark — torch on if getCapabilities().torch (via the existing torch.ts detailed apply),
exposureCompensation raised to the track's max if exposed; both restored when light is
adequate or the mode leaves Auto; in browsers with neither (his in-app webview) it is a silent
no-op and the existing 'torch = absent' note is unchanged. (3) SOFTWARE (every browser):
DIM title → the lifted standard band pooled from frame 1 under the same 900ms cap + a
lifted rescue stage (standard + tall windows with gain, after straight + deskew rescue, same
0.82 bar); DIM bottom → one lifted bottom read from frame 1 pooled into the rescue sources
(the verified-pair path untouched). The lift is applied to the RAW band pixels BEFORE the
treatment filter ( ocr.ts BandSpec.lift in buildBandCanvas) — order is load-bearing: post-
filter, the contrast crush has already zeroed the band and there is nothing left to multiply.
CRUSHED title → all title reads skipped (stage line: 'title skipped (too dark)'), bottom evidence
still runs, and a failure settles with the lighting-specific message ('Too dark to read — the
card's name area is in shadow, and auto light couldn't recover it…') via scanFailureMessage ;
Rapid surfaces TOO_DARK_RAPID_HINT once per dark streak (a ref re-armed by any non-dark
outcome). UI: the Light chip is now a 3-state cycle   💡 Auto (default) / 💡 On / 💡 Off,
persisted as manabox-light-mode (the legacy binary torch pref migrates: stored-on → On);
Off disables the whole layer including the software lift; the mode persists across camera
restarts while the physical torch state does not. The pre-existing dark-frame exposure retake
in capturePreparedBurst is untouched (capture-level, whole-card mean) — the pooled lift is
the band-level mechanism, no duplication.

Pass 19 (2026-11-09) — FULL SCANNER REWORK: image
recognition becomes the PRIMARY channel (user directive
verbatim: 'rework the whole scanner and build it better with
the image recognition fully. Make it just like manabox please'
— supersedes the Pass 18 ship-rule hold)

Architecture, three stages, each measured; all non-scanner features and all user data untouched;
every proven safeguard kept as an input to the new engine (deadline settlement, rect authority +
Pass 18 stabilisation, Auto Light, success-is-final, self-aiming bottom strip, title OCR demoted to a
fusion channel). NO hardcoded card names/IDs anywhere in the engine.

    STAGE 1 — INDEX + MATCHER, MEASURED OFFLINE ( client/src/finder.ts ,
     tests/harness/finder2.ts → results/p19-finder.json + .txt ). Fingerprint: 64-bit DCT
    pHash (32×32 grayscale → DCT-II → 8×8 low-frequency, DC excluded from the median) ×2 —
    WHOLE-CARD + ART-CROP (card x 0.055–0.945, y 0.105–0.405; ignores title bar, borders, foil)
    — Hamming distance, combined = art + card (0–128). Query path: detected card →
    perspective dewarp to the canonical 250×350 card (homography solver in finder.ts; the
    detector yields axis-aligned rects, so the dewarp is the canonical resample of the rect — the
    keystone cost is measured by the rotation classes) → fingerprint → ranked candidates. INDEX
    PLACEMENT (the honest engineering call, measured): a full ~110k-printing hash index is
    only 1.76 MB (110,000 × 16 B) BUT a linear scan costs a measured 3.4ms per 343 printings on
    this VM → ≈1.1s per query at 110k (×5 hypotheses ≈ 5.5s) — outside the 2.5s attempt deadline
    on-device-class hardware — and building it needs an offline pass over ~110k image
    downloads that cannot be rebuilt at set release. The shipped design therefore splits
    ManaBox's job: (1) a SERVER-SIDE visual index — the new identifyByImage action (vision
    model reads the dewarped card BY ARTWORK; EVERY proposal is verified against Scryfall
    server-side, so an invented name can never reach the gate; coverage = every printing Scryfall
can verify, no client download, no stale index); (2) the ON-DEVICE pHash verification — the
client fingerprints the query (multi-hypothesis) and every candidate printing's real Scryfall
reference image, and the MEASURED Hamming distance is what the gate settles on. A
proposal whose reference art doesn't match the photo cannot settle. Accuracy over the 343-
printing corpus index (shipped finder code): name top-1 8/8 identity/dim/blur/sheen/noise,
8/8 rot±3, 7/8 rot±5 (top-3 8/8 everywhere); printing top-1 8/8 identity/dim/noise, 5–7/8 under
blur/rotation/sheen. Distance distributions (gate calibration): true matches min 0 / p50 16
/ p90 36 / max 44; best impostors min 32 / p10 36 / p50 40 — the overlap band is 32–44, so
the gate combines an absolute ceiling with a margin (below). Per-query latency over 343 refs:
median 3.4ms (the shipped verification set is ≤24 printings → <1ms).
THE #77 DIAGNOSIS + FIX (measured): Pass 18's A3 failure (true card #77 there; rank 17
single-hypothesis over this run's index, d=52) was NOT hash coarseness: the A3 rect is seated
~4% too LOW (top 0.11 vs the card's 0.07), so the crop misses the card's top and carries tray at
the bottom. Insets alone measurably CANNOT fix it (they shrink further from the card —
multi-hypothesis with insets only: rank 22). The shipped hypothesis set adds UP-SHIFTS
( rectHypotheses : base, inset 5%, inset 10%, shift-up 5%, shift-up+inset): A3 → rank 1, d=34;
A2 (dim) rank 2 → 1 (d=42 → 32); A1 stays rank 1 (d=26). Best-distance-per-candidate across
hypotheses is the mechanism.
STAGE 2 — GATED FUSION ( client/src/fusion.ts , pure; tests/fusion.test.ts 20
checks). Gate constants from the Stage 1 distributions + Pass 18 points (true A1 24 / A2 34;
the false Thief settle at 48): IMAGE_GATE_DIST 40 (absolute), IMAGE_GATE_MARGIN 8 (name-level
runner-up margin), Rapid solo IMAGE_RAPID_SOLO_DIST 30 / margin 12, evidence ceiling 56.
Matrix: number hit settles as today (image never overrides a printed number); image decisive
+ token corroboration (Pass 18 rule: ≥3-letter non-stopword name token in the pooled OCR)
→ settle, Rapid auto-add eligible; image decisive alone → Snap settles, Rapid only under the
strict solo gate else PRESENTED as top Did-you-mean (never auto-added); OCR decisive +
image agrees/abstains → settle-ocr (the floor); OCR decisive + image decisive for a DIFFERENT
name → CONFLICT (no auto-add either mode; both names in Did-you-mean, conflict labelled
in diagnostics; a cheap already-landed check also blocks per-frame title wins); image present-
only merges into the uncertain suggestions — including the no-read case (pure-art band: the
band has no text but the card does). Printing (rule d): winning printing pins by art when its
printingMargin ≥ 4, else bottom-line salvage / default with diagnostics saying which ('printing
pinned by art' vs 'printings share the same art'). A3 regression pinned in tests: at d=48 the
fusion ABSTAINS in both modes (the Pass 18 false settle is impossible by construction —
corroboration is only consulted after the gate); at the post-fix d=34 it settles on the true card.
STAGE 3 — SCANNER REBUILT IMAGE-FIRST ( App.tsx identifyBurst +
server/src/actions.ts identifyByImage + scandebug.ts ). The image channel starts at
attempt start and runs CONCURRENTLY with the OCR reads (its latency hides inside the
existing deadline envelope; settlement awaits it only with the rescue envelope's remainder).
No index download at first open (server-side placement) — the only first-run cost is the first
vision call, raced at 3.5s and degrading silently to the OCR floor. Diagnostics ('What the
scanner read') now show: the DEWARPED card thumbnail ('Straightened card the image
  channel matched'), an 'Image match:' line (top-3 candidates with art+card distance splits,
  gate verdict, fusion outcome incl. CONFLICT wording), plus all pre-existing evidence/stages.
  Viewfinder UX unchanged (guide/outline/calibration/Rapid/Snap/Take Photo/Box/Foil/Auto
  Light all as before). Auto Light interplay: dim bands still lift for OCR; the pHash is contrast-
  normalised by construction (finder suite: ×0.5 dim stays at d=0 on synthetic, corpus dim class
  8/8).
  ACCEPTANCE / BATTERY: harness --set all final ( results/p19-final.json , 156 records):
  per-record diff vs p18-final = ZERO changed verdicts, ZERO regressions, 0 wrong (the
  harness pipeline mirrors the OCR/number floor, which this pass did not modify; the vision
  proposal step needs the platform inference runtime the harness cannot run, so the image

STEP 2 — FIX (the convicted branches, narrow). client/src/scanflow.ts :
IMAGE_VISION_CAP_MS = 15,000 (was the inline 3,500); IMAGE_WAIT_DEADLINE_MS = 12,000 +
fusionWaitDeadlineMs(ocrDecisive) — a DECISIVE OCR pool keeps the 4s rescue envelope
(happy-path timing byte-identical; the image channel can still veto via the conflict interlock),
an UNCERTAIN pool waits for the primary channel to the 12s deadline;
watchdogImageDeferral (pure) — the watchdog re-arms once just past a registered
sanctioned wait instead of tearing it down (settled attempts never defer; success-is-final
untouched). client/src/App.tsx : runImageChannel uses the 15s cap and keeps the three
call endings DISTINCT (race timeout vs action error — the .catch(() => null) deliberately
stays outside the race because raceWithCap maps rejections to "timeout" — vs an honest
empty list); the reference phase is PARALLEL (same bounds: ≤3 names, ≤8 printings each,
same 1.5s per-action caps, same 2.5s per-image timeout); the fusion consult registers
{attempt, untilEpochMs} in imageWaitRef during a non-decisive wait and clears it after.

ITEM B — FUSION CORROBORATION (fusion.ts). corroborationFor names three sources:
(i) TOKEN — meaningful overlap only: a ≥4-letter name token in the pooled reads, or ≥2
distinct name tokens (Pass 19's any-single-≥3-letter rule is what let 'way' corroborate the
Thief; it is gone); (ii) MATCHER-TOP — the pooled matcher's own top pick IS the image top-1;
(iii) BOTTOM — the caller scores the pooled bottom text against the candidate's printings
(existing salvage rule: set+number+artist) and passes bottomConsistent (computed in App
ONLY when (i)/(ii) haven't already corroborated, one raced printings fetch). Strength: (iii) is
strong alone; (ii) is strong ONLY with (i) — a fragment pool crowning a wrong card top by
itself is the A3 trap, not agreement; (i) alone is weak. Rules shipped in fuseChannels:
decisive tier + ANY source → settle both modes on the standard bar (P1 repaired:
matcher-top agreement auto-adds in Rapid); band tier + strong → settle both modes;
band + weak token → Snap settles, Rapid presents; band + matcher-top alone → present
(A3 guard); decisive + uncorroborated → Snap settles / Rapid needs the unchanged solo
bar; conflict interlock now also fires for band + bottom-corroborated disagreement, and
band alone never vetoes a decisive title read. Four existing fusion.test.ts assertions were
deliberately revised and re-pinned with reasons in the test comments (A3 d=48: abstain →
present-only; A3 true-card d=34 fragment case: settle → present, with a new 'mind'-fragment
variant that settles; beyond-evidence fixture 52 → 60 since 52 is now band evidence) — in

asserted.
ITEM C — WATCHDOG COMPOSITION (scanflow.ts + App.tsx). Diagnosis of P3: the Pass 20
deferral was armed ONLY while the image wait was pending; in P3 the channel had already
