settlement. Fix: watchdogShieldDeferral (pure) generalises the deferral over ALL attempt
shields; identifyBurst registers a shield at the fusion consult covering the remaining clock
envelope + 2.5s settlement slack, and the channel's completion handler records the
completion (keyed by attempt) and extends a shield by IMAGE_SETTLE_GRACE_MS (10s — the
remaining pipeline is deadline-bound by construction: cappedRead abandons a never-
returning OCR job at its cap and the Pass 15 generation cancels stale reads, so settlement
inside the grace is guaranteed unless the pipeline itself is wedged past every deadline, in
which case the expired shield lets the normal recovery run). Zombie OCR stages needed no
new mechanism — the caps + generation already bound them; what was missing was the
watchdog knowing. The rescue pass itself is UNCHANGED (zero OCR-floor risk).

ITEM C — BLOWN-BAND GUARD (scanflow.ts classifyBandBleach + ocr.ts): bands whose
sampled pixels are uniform — ink share (pixels deviating >30 from their row median) < 0.004
AND luminance stdev < 16 AND mean ≥ 60 — classify PRE-OCR as no-text evidence: blown-
white (mean ≥ 235; the P-c shape, both bands pure white under the Light chip) or uniform
(the P-a shape, empty grey gradient, mean ≈129). Title and bottom band reads short-circuit at
0ms with the diagnostics reason 'band bleached — no text', so bleached bands stop

pooled as pseudo-evidence ('thin margin'). The mean ≥ 60 floor is deliberate: uniform near-
black is the crushed/too-dark population owned by the Pass 17 Auto Light floor, NOT bleach.
Proven on fixtures (pass23.test.ts): pure white → blown-white; grey gradient → uniform;
genuine low-contrast strokes (ink share 0.166), a strong title, a half-black/white edge (stdev
≈118), and a near-black band all classify NOT bleached and still read.

pool is not itself decisive is HELD through a bounded image wait (fusionWaitBudgetMs caps it
at 8s) and settles UNLESS the image top-1 is decisive for a different card — then the scan
presents a conflict; decisive-title scans keep the historical number-wins-on-the-spot

colour-twin impostors (9–12 cells): in dim cells, quantisation at capture collapses red colour-
layout twins (Kavu Scout → Chain Lightning / Lightning Bolt at d=42–44; Last Gasp →

medians 42–50 — while ZERO bright-cell impostors reached decisive at any blur. Two-part fix:
(a) exposure-normalised query pooling — when the dewarp meters dim, gain-lifted copies
of every query crop are fingerprinted alongside the raw set (the Pass 17 lift, via
liftCanvasCopy ); measured effect is honest and modest: true-match distances tighten in
dim light (22→20, 34→32, one impostor top-1 corrected) but the wrong cells are byte-
identical — pHash bits are median-relative by construction and multiplication cannot restore
quantised-away levels, so the lift is kept for the tightening and is NOT the safety fix; (b) the
degraded-capture guard ( captureDim in fuseChannels, IMAGE_DEGRADED_MARGIN = 12 in
finder.ts): on a capture that meters dim/crushed (full-card meter, mode-independent), a
decisive verdict must ALSO clear margin ≥ 12 and, when burst frames measured one, a cross-
frame median ≤ 44 — failing either, the candidate is PRESENTED (scored 0.86, panel line says
why: 'dim capture: decisive needs margin ≥ 12 and stable frames'). Clean captures keep the
Item A rule byte-for-byte, and the user's own panels are that class (bright box, margin ∞).

auto-add 977, suggestion 246, no-read 217. The guard's cost is visible and accepted: ~56

motion-long cells largely no-read/suggestion (the information is not in the pixels); dim-class
detection at 64% under the synthetic ×0.35 (a real phone's auto-exposure is not modelled);
full-bleed 100% cards lose border-locked dewarp accuracy.

    evidence (same card d=50/42/40 across attempts) is attempt-to-attempt variance; the

Pass 24a (2026-11-10) — continuous calibration + steady light
(Items A+B only; tokens/lands, image latency and suggestions
are Pass 24b, untouched here)

Prompted by Andrew's 2026-11-10 verdict on Pass 23 (working much better — first on-device
image auto-add, Borg Queen with exact printing): (1) he must recalibrate ~every other card in his
fixed box; could the app keep calibrating continuously / re-calibrate when a new card slides in? (2)
the auto light pulses 'like a heartbeat', so a capture can land in a dark phase.

    ITEM A — LOCALISED CAUSE. The stored box calibration is ONE static rect, while cards seat
    with a few % of position variance per card. The pre-24a effective-rect rule made it worse, not
better: detections are shadow-biased in the box (Pass 18: raw detection y0 sd 0.034, range
0.077 of frame height on his rig), so a displaced card's detection repeatedly "agreed" with the
calibration inside the 4%/6% tolerances — or Pass 18 stabilisation's majority vote, whose
history fills with calibration copies once calibration reads begin, self-confirmed the stale
position; and on detection-miss frames the attempt fell back to the static calibration wherever
it pointed (his Cadet panel read a near-full-frame calibration rect (0.04,0.00)-(0.98,0.93)). The
stored position NEVER learned from detections.
ITEM A — FIX (his model: track continuously, re-lock on slide-in). New pure tracker in
detect.ts ( seedTrackedRect / observeTrackedRect / freshTrackedRect , TrackedRectState ):
seeded from the stored calibration; folds every live frame's detection into a per-edge median
of the last 5 samples (≤10s old). A sample > 0.07 from the lock on any edge only ARMS a re-
lock; a second far sample agreeing with it confirms the move (one jitter spike, rig range
0.077, can never drag the lock). prepareCardFromFrame gained trackedRect + a
detectedRect on every return (this frame's own detection, the tracker's feed): in Box mode,
when the tracked rect matches this frame's detection within 0.03 on every edge the attempt
reads the TRACKED rect ( rectOrigin: "tracked" — the median is the lower-variance
estimate of the same position); a larger gap means the card moved / the lock is still swinging
→ the attempt reads THIS frame's detection ( "frame" , no calibrationStale nag —
displacement is the normal case now); a detection miss falls back to the tracked rect
( "tracked-fallback" ), then the calibration seed. App.tsx feeds the tracker from the single
prepareCardCanvas wrapper every live box prepare uses (Rapid watcher, preview, taps,
bursts), seeds it on Box enable / recalibrate, and the viewfinder overlay prefers the live lock
('TRACKING CARD (AUTO)'). Manual     🎯 Recalibrate remains as override (it now RE-SEEDS the
tracker). Diagnostics: describePrepRect prints 'tracked (auto) (x0,y0)-(x1,y1)' / '… detection
missed this frame — last tracked position' / 'calibration (seed) (…)' (the seed label replaces the
old 'calibration (…)' string; one rectauthority assertion re-pinned). When no tracker is offered
(harness battery, stabilisation suite), prepare behaviour is the pre-24a logic verbatim.
ITEM A — PROOF. New tests/harness/trackedsession.ts → results/p24a-tracked-
session.json : 6-card box session, calibration recorded ONCE on card 1, seats displaced
+4.1/−3.3/+5.3/−4.5/+3.1% of frame height, shadow-gradient detections with rig-class jitter;
attempts judged against per-seat oracle reads. BASELINE (pre-24a call shape): 5/6 attempts
DEGRADED, 5 stale-calibration reads, all 6 attempts read the card-1 calibration, mean oracle
error 0.033 — his 'every other card' reproduced. TRACKED: 0/6 degraded, 0 stale reads,
mean oracle error 0.010 (worst 0.018), ZERO manual recalibrations.
tests/tracking.test.ts (23 checks): seed semantics, jitter-median stability, arm-then-
confirm re-lock, full session (tracked → frame-on-slide → re-locked tracked), fallback ladder,
describe strings. Pass 16 swamp scenarios stay 7/7.
ITEM B — LOCALISED CAUSE. evaluateAutoLight was driven directly by every metering tick
(preview ~800ms, Rapid watcher ~900ms, per attempt) against a SINGLE threshold (title p90 <
150 = dark) on a SELF-REFERENTIAL measurement: engaging torch/exposure brightens the
very zone being metered, so the next tick read 'bright' → restore → dark again → re-engage
— a limit cycle with no hysteresis and no dwell, i.e. the heartbeat he saw, and a capture could
    land mid-cycle in the dark state. Second actor: capturePreparedBurst 's dark-frame retry
    raised exposureCompensation mid-burst without registering it, fighting Auto's restore logic
    (and leaving EC maxed for the session).
    ITEM B — FIX (decide once per attempt, latch, hysteresis + dwell). New latch in
    autolight.ts: evidence bands DARK < 130 / BRIGHT ≥ 175 (the old 150 threshold sits inside the
    dead band — a self-lit zone metering ~150–175 can no longer release the light that's lighting
    it); stepLightLatch (preview) flips only on decisive evidence and only after a 4000ms
    minimum dwell; beginAttemptLight runs ONCE at attempt start (in identifyBurst, on the
    attempt's own metering): a decisive dark verdict engages + LATCHES ( attemptDark ), after
    which every step/begin is a strict no-op until endAttemptLight (identifyBurst finish() + both
    callers' finally for abort/watchdog paths). Preview metering may keep running but can never
    mutate an in-flight attempt. The burst dark-retry's EC raise now registers via
     noteExternalLightApplication so the latch and hardware state stay coherent. Panel line
    unchanged in wording ('zone dark — lifted ×N' / 'light good — no lift') and now states the
    LATCHED verdict. On/Manual modes untouched.
    ITEM B — PROOF. tests/lightlatch.test.ts (24 checks): evidence bands; heartbeat
    oscillation p90 138↔166 ×120 steps → ZERO applications; engaged + self-lit 155↔172 →
    ZERO releases; dwell refuses a 1s-later flip, allows after dwell; full 65↔205 swings at 1Hz →
    applications ≥ dwell apart; exactly ONE application across a simulated attempt with mid-
    attempt bright/dark/neutral steps all no-ops; attempt-start inherits preview dwell; external
    (burst-retry) applications register and hold.
    PROOF / ACCEPTANCE. Baselines on untouched code first: unit suites 683 PASS / 0 fail
    (identical count to Pass 23); battery results/p24a-baseline.json Tier 1 = 125/0/7 of 132,

Pass 27 (2026-11-10) — still-seated re-scan on a timer (Item 1),
full-art printing discrimination (Item 2)

Andrew's report (2026-11-10 ~13:30 EST): duplicates persist AFTER Pass 26 — "it just scans if I wait
about five seconds to ten seconds it just automatically scans another of the same card. It does
recognize if I slide another card in" — and the TRK #314 Swamp was actually FRA #390, a FOIL
full-art/extended-art Swamp (Daniel Romanovsky); extended-art/full-art printings must resolve to
the CORRECT printing. He asked for suggestions that would fix these: implemented what the
measurements convicted, the rest recorded as proposals with numbers.

    ITEM 1 — LOCALISATION (instrumented, suspects eliminated in order). The watcher
    ( App.tsx scanGuideSignature(sampleIntervalMs 250) ) skips sampling while an attempt is
    busy; signatures come from the guide canvas, NOT the tracker rect — so tracker drift (suspect
    a-iii) cannot move the handled comparison. The Did-you-mean resumeRapid builds a fresh
    signature (no stale reference). The 8s watchdog cannot duplicate (generation counter +
    AttemptGuard drop a killed attempt's settle; its rescue adds once). Prime suspect was suspect
    (a): the handled phase's RAW signature comparison. INSTRUMENT: tests/harness/probe-
    p27-drift.ts — the REAL RapidMachine (box mode) + the REAL light latch ( autolight.ts ) at
    the app's exact cadence model (250ms watcher tick, samples skipped while busy, preview
    metering every 3rd tick, attempt start = engage-decision/sample/set-latch, attempt end =
    release + latch clear, 3.2s attempt) over the real p26 hand-held scene, 240 simulated seconds,
    photometric model = latch-driven persistent gain steps with AE convergence (τ=900ms) ±
    slow AE wander; signatures are the App's own mirrored 24×34 function. PRE-FIX TRACE:
    Scenario A2 (dim box, light gain ×2.2 — the latch limit cycle): 46 scans of a card that never
    left — settle→re-arm gaps at latch flips with raw sceneDiff 29–76, beep-to-beep ≈5.25s, inside
    Andrew's "five to ten seconds". Scenario B (constant light, pure AE wander ±20%/25s — the
    in-app-browser case, no torch exists there): re-arm at settle+9.25s, 20 scans. Scenario A
    (×1.55 latch): 2 scans. Scenario C (steady light, noise only): exactly 1 — the machine itself is
    sound. ROOT CAUSE (one sentence): the handled change test is a RAW photometric diff
    against a signature frozen at fire time; persistent photometric STATE changes (the Pass 24a
    latched light's own engage/release; camera AE/AWB wander) accumulate across the frozen
    reference until they cross the same-card threshold (20 box) — indistinguishable from a new
    card. Pass 26's probe missed the class because every event it modelled RETURNED to baseline
photometrics within 1–3 samples; a state flip or ramp never returns. Statistic selection
measured ( probe-p27-measure.ts , kept): at the dim-box operating point mean-normalised
diff gives same-card 0.0–0.1 vs different-card ≥20.5 / removed ≥30.3 — clean separation at the
existing threshold 20; median-ratio and sqrt-median variants measured, no decisive
advantage (sqrt-median worse: 11.1 on a dim release). Known limit, recorded: a ×1.45
UPWARD step on an already-BRIGHT scene saturates highlights (normalised 24.3) —
information-theoretic (clipped cells carry no pattern); the latch only engages when the scene
is dark, so that step class cannot originate from the light system, and AE wander at ±20%
measures ≤7.3.
ITEM 1 — FIX (rapid.ts). (i) New exported signatureDiffNormalised(a, b) — rescales b to
a's mean luminance before the mean-abs diff; degenerate (≤0.5 mean) falls back to raw. The
handled phase's change comparison ( sceneDistance ) is gain-normalised; the watching
phase's noise/stability/retry logic is untouched (its 9/7/14/20 thresholds were measured raw).
(ii) The absence rule was hardened after scenario D exposed a SECOND door in the first
normalised build: normalised shape alone scored a dim seated card within threshold of the
near-structureless tray baseline. New exported signatureStructure(sig) (mean |v/mean −
1|, gain-invariant; tray ≈0.055, card ≈0.35) and matchesBaseline(sig, baseline, threshold)
— normalised shape match AND structure ≤ max(0.12, 1.8×baseline structure); absence =
matchesBaseline AND the baseline is the closer match by a margin (baseDiff + 6 ≤ sceneDiff).
(iii) The watching-phase baseline guard also uses matchesBaseline — scenario D showed the
raw guard firing a wasted attempt on the EMPTY tray mid-AE-ramp. (iv) Degenerate flat-field
fallback ( sceneDistance / matchesBaseline ): when BOTH signatures are near-structureless
(<0.02) there is no spatial signal to normalise and the decision falls back to the raw diff — the
uniform-grid fixture case (and the pre-Pass-27 rule); real trays (0.055) and cards (≥0.2) always
take the normalised path. POST-FIX PROBE (persisted results/p27-drift.txt ): A = 1 scan,
A2 = 1 (was 46), B = 1 (was 20), C = 1, D = exactly 2 — swap A→B at t=60s under latch cycle +
wander: re-arm at 60.00 (normalised diff 66.4, card gone), B fires at 62.50 (0.5s after it
stabilises), then B sits 178s with zero re-scans. Slide-out/in and swaps confirmed at the
required timings. The invariant "a card that never leaves NEVER re-scans, however long it sits"
now holds by construction for the photometric classes: the only signals that can re-arm are
spatial (a different pattern, or the baseline's own shape at baseline structure).

    (a) THE DECAY INVESTIGATION — sequence state is HEALTHY on the bench; no decay
    reproduced on easy cards. New harness tests/harness/snapsequence.ts (+ prep_p28.py
    fixtures: Griffin Protector M20 20, Hydra FRA 360, Chill FRA 297, Torch FRA 309, Treasure
    Keeper AER 177 composited in the box at six seats with variance): a faithful simulation of the
    App's session state around the real identifyBurstHarness — tracker seeding/folding via the
    production createCardTracker / prepareCardFromFrame , 3 preview ticks per card feeding the
    Pass 15 retention, retained-frame consumption, applyRectAuthority semantics,
     noteDetection miss counting, virtual clock. Six consecutive Snaps with swaps (Griffin →
    Hydra → Chill → Torch → Treasure → Griffin): all 6 identified correctly, zero degraded
    attempts, tracker followed every seat, retained frames always showed the current card,
    zero OCR worker recycles (H3's zombie class does not recur on the Snap path, including
    attempts whose predecessor ended via the image-timeout shape — the harness's image
    channel is stubbed, noted). H1 (tracker state) REFUTED under clean geometry: Snap's writes
    into tracker state are the same folds Rapid makes, and per-frame detection self-corrects
against the tracker whenever they disagree beyond TRACK_AGREE_EDGE. What the panels
actually show is therefore not a counter that rots — it is per-attempt GEOMETRY and
RETENTION failures that a refresh happens to mask (a refresh re-seeds the tracker from
calibration and clears retention, so the first attempt gets the cleanest state). Two
mechanisms were convicted on other fixtures:
(a1) CONVICTED — RETAINED FRAME NEVER RE-VALIDATED AGAINST THE LIVE SCENE
(H2). Code audit: retainedFrameRef is set by the ~800ms manual-mode preview tick,
consumed by takeRetainedFrame (2.5s TTL), cleared only after TWO consecutive detection
misses — so a fast swap, or ticks landing mid-slide / on a hand / on the empty tray, can leave
a fresh-TTL retention whose pixels show a different scene than the one being snapped, and
capturePreparedBurst led the burst with it unconditionally. Probe tests/harness/probe-p28-
stale.ts : retention = Griffin prep, live card = Chill (scene distance 28.7) — the PRE-FIX burst
shape [retained-A, B, B] reads "no readable text" (two cards' pixels pooled destroy both
reads — the panels' exact failure shape, including that the attempt fails entirely rather than
naming the previous card); a fresh [B, B, B] burst identifies Chill via FRA 297. FIX SHIPPED
(App.tsx capturePreparedBurst + rapid.ts): before a retained frame leads, ONE probe grab
is taken; retainedFrameMatchesScene compares 24×34 guide signatures with the Pass 27
gain-normalised sceneDistance (threshold RETAINED_SCENE_MAX_DISTANCE = 14; measured
separation on rig fixtures: same scene ≈ 0–7, card swap ≥ 28.7, card→empty tray 43.2, pure
gain change ≈ 0). On a match the probe becomes burst frame 2 (no capture wasted, retention
still leads — Pass 15 behaviour preserved); on a mismatch the retention is dropped and the
probe leads a normal fresh burst; a failed probe grab keeps the historical unconditional
behaviour. Net per-Snap state change: a retained frame is now consumed only when it
provably shows the current scene; everything else about Snap state (tracker folds, detection
authority, worker generations) is unchanged and was measured healthy.
(a2) CONVICTED — DETECTOR SLAB MERGES THE BOX'S RECESS SEAMS INTO THE CARD
SPAN (the panels' crop geometry). Fixtures tests/harness/prep_p28b.py (deep box, soft
seams) and .cache/p28hard (deep box, HARD recess seams + a tray-floor seam), same seats
as the flat set. Soft seams: geometry perfect, all 5 cards identified fresh. HARD seams: Griffin
and Chill detections ran to the recess corner (raw x0=0.041 = the recess edge) — read-rect
topCut −0.185 / −0.169, title centroid at 0.23 / 0.22 of the crop, beyond every title window incl.
rescue bands (reach ≈0.19); full pipeline ( probe-p28-hardpipe.ts ): Griffin = "no readable
text" with debris reads (the panel-1 shape exactly), Chill's title dead the same way (its bottom
line rescued it in the bench run; on the phone the opposite merge direction bleached the
bottom instead — same defect, other end). Mechanism: the recess seam band + the tray gap
fit inside the span finder's 8% gap-merge tolerance, so the slab bridges from the card to the
recess walls; borderless/edge-blended cards are the vulnerable class. A "void-bridge ban" in
longestRun was considered and REJECTED — small cards' smooth art rows sit below the
rough threshold and rely on the same bridging (Pass 9 Treasure Keeper lesson). FIX SHIPPED
( trimSeamBand , detect.ts): after span selection (refined spans, and the rough fallback), each
span edge is trimmed when it shows the seam signature — a dense band ≤5.5% of the axis
(thickness is a frame property, not a span property), then a SUSTAINED void (≥ max(6, 3% of
span) samples below 35% of the span's interior-median density, judged on a ±3 max-filtered
profile so double-line seams and ≤5-row in-card art dips don't confuse it), then the sustained
mass; bounded to 22% of the span per edge (the convicted fixtures trim 13–14%; pickSpan's
55% discipline untouched). A real card edge never has a void behind its border line — the
title/text mass is contiguous — so the trim only removes detached seams. Post-fix geometry:
Griffin topCut −0.030, Chill −0.029, title centroids 0.11; pipeline: Griffin identifies (M20 20),
Chill identifies (FRA 297); flat and soft-seam sets byte-comparable (all title centroids 0.09–
0.12 before and after).
(b) HARD-FRAME VERDICTS (measured separately, not shipped as decay fixes). Guiding
Hydra (FRA 360, borderless title-over-art): geometry is FINE on every seam variant (topCut ≈
−0.02); its title channel reads nothing usable even when settled (translucent banner over busy
art — the pooled title reads are debris); it settles via the bottom line ("R 0360 FRA" → FRA 360)
when that read lands, and repeated identical-input attempts flip between settle and no-read
with byte-identical rects ( probe-p28-hydra.ts : 4/5 settled) — the variance is read-stage
timing under load, not framing. On the phone its panel-1 failure is the (a2) crop (the

    S2/S4 REFUTED in code and sim. The Rapid watcher path DOES apply Pass 28's retained-
    frame scene probe (same capturePreparedBurst ); panels printing "frame: retained" is the

    duplicate). With a mid-slide HOLD (the slide pauses ~900ms while the user reaches for the
    next card), the held partial scene is perfectly stable, so the machine fired on it in BOTH slide

( presentationComplete , detect.ts: centre displacement ≤10% of frame on both axes, height
ratio 0.86–1.16 — his seating variance is 3–6% and the calibrationStale low-seat class at 8%
still passes; a 20%-of-travel hold fails at 0.15, the 45% holds at 0.34) and hands incomplete
fires back via machine.deferScan() (returns to watching with fire-point stability kept and the
attempt budget unspent; the App caps consecutive deferrals at 8 ≈ 2s so a misjudged seat
can never wedge Rapid). The --probeup measurement added a second trigger: a partial slab
touching the frame edge yields NO detection at all (detectInCanvas null at all three hold

below the box same-card threshold of 20 (at gain 1.0 the same pair reads 26.0; dim light
compresses dark cards together; same-card noise is 0.3), while every transition/empty scene
during the same swap measures 33.4–41.5. Pre-fix sim: after a 400ms or 600ms swap
completed under the sheet and the user picked A, the machine believed A was still seated
and B was NEVER attempted (B added ×0, deterministic at both durations). FIX SHIPPED: the
watcher keeps feeding the machine SUPPRESSED samples while the sheet is up ( sample(...,
{ suppressed: true }) — handled-phase departure rules and watching-phase stability run, a
scan decision is held with stability pinned at the fire point, due retries are held too), so the
swap is OBSERVED as a transition, not inferred from endpoints; resumeRapid now calls
machine.resumeFromPause(sig) , which keeps the re-armed watching state when a departure
was observed ( departed flag, set by the handled phase's own change/absence rules) and

the pick and settles (both durations). Documented residual (unit-pinned): a swap whose
transition falls entirely between two watcher ticks (sub-~250ms, under the sheet) still
endpoints-compares below threshold and stays handled — the latch needs one observed
transition tick, the same limit the live path has always had. Pause-after swaps (slide after the
pick) were already clean and remain so.
ITEM 2(a) — FOIL SHIMMER vs FRAME STABILITY: NOT CONVICTED on synthetic evidence;

the TRUE card in 96/96 triples and CONSTANT across frames in 96/96; per-frame spread stays

Corroborating, from the persisted Pass 23 matrix (3 runs × 1,440 cells): among 194 dim
decisive-consensus cells, ALL 9 impostor winners sit at margin 8–10 (the margin leg alone
demotes every one) and ZERO correct winners with margin ≥12 had median >44 — the

synthetic dim fixtures). "Looks like its having trouble with bad lighting. The light isnt working
please fix it. Make sure the auto lighting works. The on light works. The manual from off low
medium and high but have a slider so i can gradually increase it and decrease it." Both attempts

both took the full 12s image-timeout path, so the OCR floor carried alone. Localisation fixtures:

composites at his box seat under a low-right light pool / uniform dim), probes probe-p30-

    ITEM A — TRACKED-RECT COLLAPSE: CONVICTED, mechanism + guard shipped.
    Reproduction on production code ( probe-p30-dark.ts , pre-fix): in the dark scene the
    detector returns ONLY the lit text box — raw (0.30,0.52)-(0.79,0.84) at confidence 0.84,
    corrected (0.30,0.40)-(0.80,0.90), covering 40% of the true card (his panel: (0.39,0.40)-
    (0.81,0.81)). Audit answers: (i) tracker ingestion validated NOTHING — the seeded branch of
     observeTrackedRect replaced the stored calibration with the disagreeing quadrant on the
    FIRST preview tick (no confirmation at all), and the re-lock branch adopted any two agreeing
    far detections, which a persistent dark quadrant satisfies trivially; (ii) the Pass 24a "verify the
    lock against the current frame" step checks only lock↔detection AGREEMENT (≤0.03 edge
    delta) — a quadrant agrees with itself perfectly; (iii) prepareCardFromFrame 's tracked-fallback
    read the tracked rect with no plausibility check, and the App's preview tick set "✓ Card found
— fine at this distance" from the raw quadrant detection. The chain reproduced end-to-end: 5
preview ticks → lock = quadrant (cover 0.40); Snap frame detects nothing → read origin
tracked-fallback, cover 0.40, title never in the crop. FIX ( rectPlausibleVsSeat +
effectiveSeatRef , detect.ts): a candidate rect is plausible as the card at the seat only with
area ratio 0.6–1.6 of the seat AND ≥55% seat coverage (measured separation: quadrant 0.42 /
0.39; legitimate displaced/low seats 0.86–1.15 / ≥0.82; the top-amputated detection class at
0.86 area stays plausible — the seam-trim/aim passes own it, and the merged-slab class stays
plausible for the same reason). The gate is applied at all three ingestion points:
prepareCardFromFrame treats an implausible detection as a miss (it is also withheld from
detectedRect , so it can never be folded), observeTrackedRect refuses implausible
detections entirely (no fold, no re-lock arm, no freshness refresh — judged against the
calibration while the lock matches it, else against the lock itself via effectiveSeatRef , so a
legitimately re-locked seat is not frozen), and the tracked-fallback branch only serves a
tracked rect that is itself plausible against the calibration, else the chain falls through to the
calibration seat. The App passes the stored calibration into observeTrackedRect as the seat,
gates the manual preview tick's found-card/retention on the same predicate, and flags a
surviving implausible lock in the live view (amber outline, "TRACKING — OUTLINE TOO
SMALL, CHECK LIGHT", hint replaced — never "fine at this distance"). Post-fix probe: every
dark tick reads the seat (origin tracked-fallback, cover 1.00), the lock never leaves the seat,
and the no-detection Snap reads the seat. Pipeline proof ( probe-p30-pipe.ts ): B0 = the
quadrant crop itself (what P2 read) → no-read/tooDark; B1a = Snap frame + a poisoned
quadrant tracker in state → origin calibration, the seat; B1b identical.
ITEM B — LIGHT PIPELINE AUDIT (measured answers). (1) STILL vs VIDEO pooling: the
pooling is ONE code path — Snap (video or still), Rapid and Take Photo all consume the
same identifyBurst , which pools the lifted title specs (standard + standard-low) and the
lifted bottom spec from frame 1 under the same caps (S1's harness record: liftedTitleReads=2,
liftedBottomReads=1). P2's suspiciously short 787ms title stage is explained by the crop, not
missing pooling: the quadrant's bands are tiny, so garbage reads return fast — and the lift it
pooled was computed for the quadrant's pixels (Item A's crop). One real still-path amplifier,
now moot: pre-fix the quadrant head (height 0.41 < 0.45, source "detected") triggered the hi-
res STILL retry, so P2's whole burst became still quadrant crops; post-fix the head is the seat
(0.80) and no spurious retry fires. (2) P4's shape on the bench: THE PIPELINE READS IT. S1
(Swamp ONE 274, full-card rect, title zone p90 = 74, Auto lift ×2.4): confident — Swamp ONE
274, landed by the bottom line ("274 … ONE EN" → collector-number pin), title reads "…of

evidence does not convict a specific tighter threshold. Preview metering vs the attempt latch
(the P1 "exposure was raised and the card re-captured" note): that note is the capture dark-
frame retry, which fires during capture, BEFORE the attempt latch is taken, and registers itself
via noteExternalLightApplication — the attempt-start decision cannot immediately undo it
(dwell), and stepLightLatch is a strict no-op while an attempt holds the latch (unit-proven
below). No fight; no change. (3) AUTO END-TO-END (unit/harness level, stated as such):
tests/pass30.test.ts drives the production latch + lift decisions through a scripted dark
session — dark preview (p90 50) engages by itself ( engage ), the attempt latches it, wild
preview swings mid-attempt change nothing, a bright tick inside the 4s dwell does NOT
release (no heartbeat), bright + dwell elapsed releases exactly once; the lift side reproduces
both panels' own numbers (p90 50 → ×3.5; p90 71 → ×2.5). (4) ON: CONVICTED GAP, FIXED.
Pre-fix, liftForMode("on") was the Auto metering gate verbatim (bright → NO lift at all; dim
→ the metered gain, not the cap; crushed → no lift + title skipped) and the hardware side
forced only the torch, never exposure compensation — in the read path On did nothing Auto
didn't, which is exactly what "the on light isn't working" looked like from his side of the glass.
Post-fix On is an override like Manual, at the cap: liftForMode("on") = MAX_LIFT_GAIN (×4)
on every band, the crush skip no longer applies in On (App + harness mirror), the panel line
reads "light on — lifted ×4.0 (title zone p90 = N)", and the hardware path
( applyForcedLightHardware , shared with Manual) forces torch + exposure compensation at
max on mode entry and on every track (re)start. (5) THE HONEST CEILING, with numbers:
software lift multiplies the photons already captured; it cannot create new ones. Measured
classes (Pass 17 + this pass): title zone p90 ≥ ~32 → the lift recovers readable titles (B2's seat
crop at p90 39 read its title under ×4); p90 ≤ 26 (crushed) → lifted reads manufacture
confident garbage, so Auto settles tooDark honestly instead — B1b's seat crop of the darkest
fixture meters p90 11 and settles tooDark even with the rect fixed. His panels' attempts
metered p90 50 / 71 on their (wrong or displaced) crops — INSIDE the recoverable band — so
with the rect anchored at the seat, Auto's lift is expected to carry his dark room; but a frame
as dark as the p90-11 composite is beyond software in ANY browser: the only cure is photons,
and in his in-app browser the torch does not exist (the panel copy keeps saying so, with the
Chrome note). In Chrome, Auto/On engage the real torch and the ceiling moves with it.
Fixture observation, recorded not tuned: B2 (Borg Queen at p90 39, ×4 lift) settled
"Manifest" (tsoc 30) from partial title reads ("…Queen, Perfection Manifest") — the pooled
matcher's subset scoring crowned the one-token name over the four-token full name.

sequences) over the production modules.

    ITEM A — THE TITLE STAGE, TRACED (which reads, when, why it closed). (a) THE POOL:
    only the frame loop's per-frame reads — one aimed standard-band read per burst frame (×3).
    Each returned FAST with LONG garbage (E1 'EE Wve pre ERP SN Sp -', E2 '9 eg a We Lbs as
    tits'): long enough that ocrBand 's shape heuristics (garbage <3 letters / fragmented token
    soup / thin <8 letters) did NOT trigger its PSM 6 pooling pass, so each frame cost a single PSM
    7 pass (~70–110ms on the phone) and the stage totalled 211/240/339ms. S1's 339ms is the
    same shape ending in no-readable-text (match 0ms — its pool never scored a candidate). The
    matcher RAN on E1/E2 (match 1.8s): pooledMatches re-scores the whole 35k index per new
    pool signature, so 1.8s of match over a garbage pool is expected arithmetic, not a second
    defect. A full burst title stage that same evening (Swamp, Pass 30 batch) ran ~2.0s because its
    reads took the slow paths (PSM 6 pooling + lifted copies — that attempt had Auto engaged).
    (b) RETAINED FRAME: it LED frame 1 in all three attempts (every panel: "frame: retained"). Its
    read was the pool's first evidence; Pass 28 validates its SCENE, nothing validated its FOCUS
    (see Item B). It could not settle alone (garbage is never decisive) and did not dominate a
    verdict — but a soft retained frame's garbage is pooled at equal weight with settled frames'
    reads. (c) THE CONVICTED STEP — RESCUE-2 STARVED BY THE IMAGE WAIT'S CLOCK. The
    Pass 26 completing read DID run (one tall multi-line read, here on the last frame; on these
    defocused/foil-sheened bands it also returned garbage and pooled). Snap's Rescue-2 pass
    (offset+4/+8, tall, left-shift, deskew ×4, lifted where owed) is scheduled in identifyBurst
    AFTER settlement's image fusion wait, on the SAME AttemptClock . The wait consumed its
    sanctioned budget (IMAGE_WAIT_DEADLINE_MS 12s — every panel's "Last scan: 12.0s"); the
    rescue then called clock.extendTo(ATTEMPT_RESCUE_DEADLINE_MS = 4000) , which cannot
    widen a clock already ~12–14s elapsed (extendTo only widens to an absolute budget), leaving
    remaining ≈ 0 — so EVERY rescue cappedRead returned skipped without starting ( if
    (rw.skipped) break rescueBands ). The treatments existed, were scheduled, and never ran;
    the title stage therefore stayed at the frame loop's 211–339ms. Pinned arithmetically in
    pass31.test.ts A1/A2 (extendTo(4000) after a 12s wait leaves 0ms; a starved clock skips reads
    without starting them; a hung read under a granted budget is still abandoned at cap — the
    deadline guarantee is untouched). (d) NO read-count early exit exists — the frame loop
    always ran all 3 frames; the stage closed fast because the reads were single fast passes AND
    the post-wait rescue was starved. Settling fast on a CLEAN decisive read is unchanged and
    correct; the defect was settling on garbage because the remaining treatments were
    unreachable. FIX SHIPPED: AttemptClock.extendByFromNow(ms) (scanflow.ts) +
     RESCUE_AFTER_WAIT_BUDGET_MS = 3500: Rescue-2 is granted a fresh ~3.5s budget from the
    moment it starts, whatever the image wait consumed. The fusion wait, the interlock order,
    the per-read caps and the watchdog shields are untouched; Rapid never enters Rescue-2
    (unchanged). Bench consequence: E1/E2/S1-shaped attempts now actually execute the
    offset/tall/deskew treatment set before the pool is judged — on the phone fixtures (dim + foil
sheen + Light OFF) whether those treatments read these two titles is exactly the phone-only
retest below; no bench fixture can reproduce his foil-under-box-light bands honestly, and
none was fabricated for the claim.

focus constraint (track starts in the browser's default mode); applyCameraLocks ran only at

blurred, so the metric reads focus, not light). The completing read now runs on the SHARPEST
burst frame (was: last frame); Rescue-2 reads frames sharpest-first (a decisive read stops the
pass before soft frames spend the budget); the retained frame may lead a burst only when it
is not dramatically softer than the live probe beside it ( retainedSharpnessGate , ratio 0.6 — a
pre-settle retained frame is dropped to a fresh burst, with the reason published; unmeasured
scores keep historical behaviour). Every attempt's panel gains a Focus line ("frame
sharpness: a · b · c (sharpest #N; frame 1 is the retained viewfinder frame)" + any retained-
drop reason) — the measured retained-vs-burst comparison, on every future screenshot.

monotonically in the harness and the settled frame wins selection (pass31.test.ts B3).

   ITEM 1 — THE WATCHDOG COMPOSITION, LOCALISED + CLASS AUDIT. The watchdog's
   deadline was NOT a function of the attempt's sanctioned budget: it consulted three point-
registered shields ( imageWaitRef , imageShieldRef , imageCompletedRef +
IMAGE_SETTLE_GRACE_MS ). The W arithmetic, from the code: the image wait times out at ~12s;
identifyBurst then registers the fusion shield as Date.now() + clock.remaining()(≈0) +
2500 ≈ 14.5s; fusion consults (~1.5s — the panels' match times); Rescue-2 starts (~13.5s) via
Pass 31's extendByFromNow(+3500) → sanctioned reads run to ~17s with NO shield covering
them (no image completion → no grace shield either). The watchdog's re-armed fire lands
~15s, finds every shield expired, and kills the attempt MID-RESCUE with its stages complete —
the panels' exact shape. Third sanctioned wait the watchdog failed to defer to (Pass 20/21:
the image wait; Pass 21: the completed-result grace; now: the rescue budget). SANCTIONED-
WAIT AUDIT (every extension in an attempt × its watchdog treatment): frame-loop reads
+ completing read (inside ATTEMPT_DEADLINE_MS 2500 on the AttemptClock) — inside the 8s
base before, clock-derived deadline after; bottom reads + number-rescue lookups (per-read
caps ~1.2s, lookups raced ≤1200ms, inside the clock) — same; fusion image wait extendTo
4000 decisive / 8000 number-hit cap / 12000 uncertain — image-wait deferral (uncertain
budget only) + fusion shield before, clock deferral covering all three budgets by construction
after; the image CALL itself (IMAGE_VISION_CAP_MS 15000, never awaited past the wait; its
completion handler publishes the result + buys grace) — completion-grace shield, unchanged
(anchored to the channel's report time, which the read clock does not model — the reason
shields remain as the second layer); post-image settlement (IMAGE_SETTLE_GRACE_MS 10000
from the report) — grace shield, unchanged; fusion consult tail (shield = remaining + 2500) —
unchanged, and the clock margin now grants the same tail independently; Rescue-2
extendByFromNow(RESCUE_AFTER_WAIT_BUDGET_MS = 3500) — NO COVERAGE AT ALL before
(the W1/W2 kill); covered by the clock deferral after (in the W replay the deadline
becomes start + 17000 + 2500); extendedBottomPin (one read capped by
clock.remaining() ) — inside the clock; settlement lookups inside identifyBurst
( resolveByName uncapped await, salvage raced 1500ms, pin lookups raced 1200ms — all
bounded by their own races or by the pipeline's settlement, which the guard watches); the
slow read ( identifyCore — no AttemptClock; per-variant OCR caps + one server read; runs
under the PREVIOUS attempt's guard, so a settle makes the watchdog ignore it by the
success-is-final rule) — unchanged, enumerated for completeness; the watchdog's own
evidence rescue ( raceWithCap 3000, inside the handler) — n/a. FIX (class, not instance):
identifyBurst PUBLISHES the attempt's clock ( attemptClockRef : attempt id + epoch start; the
budget is read LIVE off the clock object at every firing), and the watchdog defers to
watchdogClockDeadlineMs = epochStart + clock.budget + WATCHDOG_SETTLE_MARGIN_MS (2500
— the same tail the fusion shield always granted) whenever that deadline is in the future
( watchdogClockDeferral , pure, scanflow.ts). The watchdog now fires only when NO live
source (clock, wait, shields) has sanctioned work outstanding; any future sanctioned
extension widens the watchdog automatically because the budget IS the deadline's input.
finish() still settles the guard immediately, short-circuiting every deferral (settled → no
deferral, action "ignore", empty recovery plan — settle-twice is impossible: the watchdog's
rescue branch requires !guard.isSettled , and the guard's settle is idempotent).
REGRESSION (tests/pass32.test.ts, production functions only): the W composition
replayed on a fake clock (wait extendTo 12000 → timeout, fusion shield 14.5s, rescue grant at
13.5s → budget 17000): at the 14.75s firing the PRE-FIX SHIELD SET ALONE returns
defer=false (the kill, asserted as documentation) while the clock deferral defers to 19.5s and
the attempt settles normally at ~18.5s with success final; the INVERSE also pinned — an
unextended attempt trips at the base firing, an extended-but-dead attempt defers at 8s and
trips just past its extended deadline, another attempt's clock never defers, and a hung read
under the rescue grant is still abandoned at its cap.
ITEM 2 — SHARPNESS FLOOR, MEASURED + AF STATE. METHOD:
tests/harness/prep_p32.py rendered four real cards (Stingerquill Annex FRA 190, Eye of Jace
FRA 170, Rank Rat FRA 61, Griffin Protector M20 20; Scryfall art crops in production card
geometry) through Gaussian defocus radii 0–3.2, each also dimmed ×0.45 as a lighting
control (40 fixtures); tests/harness/probe-p32-floor.ts scored every fixture with the
PRODUCTION measureTitleSharpness and read its title zone with the production standard
band against the full 36,519-entry name index ( results/p32-floor.json ). FINDINGS: bright
ladders stay USABLE (unique decisive/usable crown = the true name; garbled reads score 0
and crown nothing) down to sharpness 18.5–22.3 per card and FAIL at ≤20.7 (defocus garbles
the title into a WRONG decisive crown — "Tree of Tales" 0.582 over Eye of Jace at 19.1 —
worse than no read); the dim controls (×0.45, unblurred) measure only 13.4–15.2 and ALL
read usable (3 of 4 decisive) — on real card art the metric is brightness-sensitive (dimming
×0.45 roughly halves it — Stingerquill 33.8 → 15.2), unlike Pass 31's synthetic pattern (dim-
sharp 36.8), so the floor had to be set in the DIM operating regime, not the bright one;
dim+soft measures 10.8–12.5 and is a coin flip (2 of 4 fail); the W bursts meter 5.9–10.3.
SHARPNESS_FLOOR = 12 (focus.ts): the highest value that never gates a readable dim-sharp
burst — a floor at the bright transition (18–22) would gate dim-but-readable cards, a
disguised LIGHT gate, forbidden — while gating every W frame. Frames between 12 and the
bright transition still get the full pipeline with sharpest-first order; the gate refuses only
bursts with NO frame at/above 12. BEHAVIOUR ( burstFocusVerdict +
captureBurstWithFocusGate , App.tsx; both capture sites — Snap's captureFromCamera and
Rapid's two burst paths): an all-soft burst (measured frames only; 0 = unmeasured keeps
historical behaviour and NEVER gates) → re-trigger autofocus ( retriggerAutofocus : a single-
shot application where that's the plan; on continuous tracks a single-shot cycle is fired and
the plan re-asserted — best-effort, capability-guarded) → wait FOCUS_SETTLE_MS = 600
(inside the brief's 400–700ms band) → ONE fresh re-capture (no retained frame) → if still all-
soft: Snap settles FAST via presentOutOfFocus — a real failed-scan panel with the
straightened image, the Focus numbers, the gate's own note ("frames out of focus — … re-
capture after refocus still soft …") and "Those frames were out of focus…" — no 3s garbage
burn, no watchdog death; Rapid quietly hands the burst back with a once-per-soft-streak hint
("Frames are arriving out of focus — …"). One re-capture max per attempt; the gate adds ≤
~1.5s (settle beat + one burst) and NO clock extension — it runs entirely before identifyBurst
creates the AttemptClock, so Item 1's deadline needs no term for it (the watchdog's subject is
an identification attempt; a gated capture never starts one). AF STATE IN DIAGNOSTICS:
applyCameraLocks records the requested focus mode ( focusRequestedRef ); every panel's
Focus line now appends describeAfState(...) — the track's exposed focusMode values, the
requested mode, and the mode getSettings() reports applied — or, when the browser
exposes no focusMode capability at all, the line SAYS so: "camera focus: not exposed by this
browser (no focusMode capability) — autofocus can be neither requested nor verified here".
Pinned in pass32.test.ts for the absent / empty / full / unreported configurations. WHAT THE
SANDBOX CANNOT DETERMINE (stated plainly): whether Andrew's in-app WebView
exposes focusMode, and whether it honoured Pass 31's continuous-AF request — the code
paths are pinned, and his next panels will print the answer on the Focus line itself.

ITEM 3b — FOIL FLAG AUDIT (trace + live contract test; NO mis-map found; one real gap
fixed). TRACE: the Rapid chip is the batchFoil state; every settle path computes foil =
(bottom-line separator star/bullet detection) ?? batchFoil — Rapid auto-add
( runRapidScan 's outcome.info.foilDetected ?? batchFoil; the watcher effect's deps include
batchFoil so no stale closure), Snap (identifyFromCardCanvas, trySlowRead), Did-you-mean
picks (selectMatch), "use this artwork match anyway", and the watchdog rescue (via
batchFoilRef , kept in sync by an effect) — then addToList(card, foil, ...) →
api.addToScanList({ foil }) → the row (server merge key = scryfallId + foil, so a foil add
and a non-foil add are separate rows) → the result sheet renders the ROW's flag verbatim ( ★
Foil: on/off , and the row price switches on the same flag). LIVE CONTRACT (sandbox
actions): addToScanList with foil:true returned a row with foil=true; a second foil:true add
MERGED (quantity 2) with foil still true (test row removed afterwards) — chip-on → foil row is
proven end-to-end at the action layer. THE WINNING ADD'S OWN EVIDENCE: in Andrew's
19:49 live view (1000044729), cropped ×3, the Foil chip reads "★ Foil" with NO ✓ and inactive
styling, while Auto-add beside it is gold WITH ✓ — the render is ★ Foil{batchFoil ? " ✓" :
""} on the active-chip style, so the batch toggle was OFF for that session. The sheet's "Foil:
off" is therefore the honest record of adds made with the batch flag off, and quantity 2 = two
foil-off adds of the same card merged (had either add carried foil on, it would have formed its
own row). THE ONE REAL GAP: batchFoil was the only batch-scoped preference NOT
persisted — light mode, manual gain, box mode and the calibration all survive a reload; the
foil toggle silently reset to off on any reload. It now persists ( manabox-batch-foil , read at
init, written on change). Merge semantics UNCHANGED (no evidence against them).

(a) NO PRE-EMPTIVE REFUSAL — THE FLOOR IS DEMOTED TO A SOFT TRIGGER
( softAttemptPlan , focus.ts; pinned in tests/pass33.test.ts). Every Snap/Rapid attempt now

sharpest-first as it already did. softAttemptPlan(scores, {snap}) is the single decision the

the ladder to gate ANY spend on this metric; the Pass 32 clock-derived watchdog deadline is

(b) STILL-CAPTURE ESCALATION ( escalateSoftBurstToStill , App.tsx). When both video
bursts meter below the floor, Snap escalates ONCE to captureStillGuideCanvas() (full-
sensor ImageCapture, no whole-frame downscale — the Pass 9 small-card path), prepares
the still like any frame (own detection/rect/sharpness), measures its title sharpness on the
SAME metric, and APPENDS the measurement to the panel's focus note ("hi-res still
escalation: still sharpness X vs video best Y — still leads the read"). The still leads the burst
when sharper than every video frame, else joins as an extra frame; the full pipeline then runs
on the burst including the still. The attempt plan is recomputed on the FINAL burst, so a
sharp still clears the soft verdict and its advice with it. Where ImageCapture doesn't exist (his
in-app browser may be one — grabFrame was already capability-guarded), the note says so
plainly ("hi-res still escalation unavailable — this browser exposes no ImageCapture stills")
and the video burst proceeds. Rapid takes no stills (cadence); its artwork channel carries soft
bursts. Whether stills actually break the softness pattern in his rig is now a measurable
phone-only question — the panel prints the still's number next to the video numbers on the
first soft Snap.
(c) METRIC RE-GROUNDING (focus.ts, documented at SHARPNESS_FLOOR ). Labelled
evidence, all of it: 11.3–11.6 real frames = artwork-identifiable + human-legible (OCR never
attempted post-gate — unknown, not failed); dim bench controls 13.4–15.2 = OCR usable;
dim+soft bench 10.8–12.5 = OCR coin flip (and the recorded ladder data, re-checked in

them); W bursts 5.9–10.3 = OCR unreadable, artwork UNTESTED there (image service timed
out — NOT claimed identifiable); Esper 2.3–4.7 = genuinely murky even to a human.
Conclusion recorded in the code: the metric supports a RELATIVE softness warning and an
escalation trigger; it does NOT support an absolute refusal — no labelled sample establishes
a score below which no channel can identify a card, and the artwork channel's tolerance was
never measured against this metric at all. SHARPNESS_FLOOR stays 12 as the trigger (its value
now only costs escalation work — an AF re-capture and possibly a still — never an attempt),
and the panel keeps printing raw numbers.
(d) THE PANEL IMAGE — FINDING + FIX. What P1 displayed was
dewarpPreparedCard(preps[0].card) : the DEWARP OF THE CROP, and the crop came from
the tracked-fallback rect on a frame whose own detection missed ("detection missed this
frame — last tracked position"). Box-mode fallback rects (tracked / calibration) are the ONE
rect class never passed through correctCardAspect at crop time — every live detection is
aspect-corrected before cropping, the fallback chain crops its rect exactly as it sits — so a lock
sitting shorter than a card's shape for its width (the Esper sits LOW in the tray per P3, its
bottom line at the tray edge) clips the card in the crop, and the old gate panel showed that
partial crop as if it were the whole capture, with no full frame anywhere for comparison and
no printed finding. The P1 detection miss itself correlates with the frame's darkness (no
exposure-raised re-capture note on that attempt; the live view detects the same card fine in
P3) — the Pass 28/30 seam family, already gated by seat plausibility; NOT separately localised
to a new defect this pass, and no speculative fix shipped for it. FIXES: (1) every attempt's
panel now ALSO publishes the full captured frame with the read rect outlined ( frameUrl
in scandebug.ts, buildScanPreview(preps[0]) published synchronously in identifyBurst
beside the dewarp; rendered in ScanDebugBlock as "Full frame as captured — the gold
outline is the area that was read") — a clipped crop can now be seen against the whole card
as captured; (2) rectClipFinding (detect.ts, pure, unit-tested) computes what the aspect
correction WOULD have covered beyond the used rect and describePrepRect appends it to
the Rect line when the missing coverage is material (>3% of frame height): "read area is
shorter than a card's shape for its width — the crop may clip the card's bottom (aspect-
corrected bottom edge would sit at Y)". Card-shaped rects (the normal detected/calibration
case) produce no finding, and the established line prefixes are byte-unchanged (the
rectauthority suite's startsWith/includes checks all still pass).

Long-term data behavior- Refresh policy: Card-names index
cached client-side until user taps Refresh (or first load).
Collection/decks are server DB source of truth, refetched via
react-query on open and invalidated on writes. Card prices are
fetched at add time and stored on the collection row
(purchase/scan-time price snapshot); collection value sums
stored foil-aware prices.

  Growth: collection_items rows (one per scryfall_id+foil+condition), decks + deck_cards rows.
  Duplicate scan of same card+foil increments quantity.
  Ordering: Collection newest first by default, searchable/filterable. Decks list by updated date.
  Time semantics: downloaded-at date for names index shown date-only; collection added_at
  stored as timestamp, rendered viewer-local.


Rejected approaches

  Tried: Downloading Scryfall bulk JSON (all cards full data, hundreds of MB) Why rejected: Far
  too large for phone/browser storage; the names catalog (~<1MB) gives instant local matching
  and full details need only one fetch per identified card.
  Tried: Client-side Tesseract.js in the browser Why rejected (v1–Pass 2): The web-artifact
  platform requires all model/vision work to run through server actions ( ctx.inference );
  client-side ML libraries are not permitted in this runtime. The title-band-only crop, pooled
  treatments and no-veto matching from the original spec are preserved exactly — only the
  reader itself moved server-side. Matching of the read text against the local name index still
  happens 100% on-device and instantly. REVERSED in Pass 3 (2026-11-08) by explicit user
  direction after timed evidence (16–20s/card) proved the server vision read is the bottleneck:
on-device Tesseract.js is now the primary path and the server read is the fallback only.
Recorded here so the reversal is not mistaken for an oversight.
Tried: Largest-centred-rectangle crop / whole-card OCR / confidence gates Why rejected:
Proven failure modes from AetherForge — diverged from guide box, debris vetoed correct
reads. Guide-mapped crop + title-band-only + no veto implemented instead.
